# NP-14 — ENGINE THEORY: Provable Claims and Use Cases

**Project Northpaw design vault · Foundation of `engine/greybox/sim/core.gd` · PCUC discipline: every claim names its test, and the test has been run**

The greybox v1–v5 proved the design. This document is the foundation the engine is rebuilt on (greybox v6): each section states a claim, the mathematics or mechanism behind it, where the industry ceiling sits and why, the executable test in this repo, and **the result of running it**. A claim without a test is not in this document; a claim whose test has not been run is marked as such.

Status legend: **PROVEN** = test run in-container, GREEN, reproduction command below. **MODEL** = a consequence of the model that no external source was found to confirm or deny; it stands until playtest data speaks.

---

## C1 — Deterministic fixed-tick simulation — PROVEN

**Claim.** A combat simulation advanced in fixed ticks (120 Hz, Δt = 1/120 s) from an input log, using no wall-clock reads and a seeded RNG, produces identical state for identical input logs regardless of render frame rate.
**Why it matters.** Frame data (a 160 ms jab is 19.2 ticks; the engine rounds to 19) only *means* something when the tick is the unit. Determinism is the precondition for replays, golden-master regression tests, and rollback netcode — the architecture every serious fighting game since GGPO runs on: simulate from inputs, never from time ([GGPO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GGPO), [rollback architectures](https://www.snapnet.dev/blog/netcode-architectures-part-2-rollback/), [determinism requirements](https://docs.coherence.io/manual/advanced-topics/competitive-games/determinism-prediction-rollback)).
**Mechanism.** `TNBSim.step(intent)` is the only way time passes. `TNBClock` (`sim/clock.gd`) turns frame deltas into a tick count and an interpolation alpha; `main.gd` draws between the last two ticks. Every ms constant in `combat_tuning.v1.json` becomes ticks at load (`ms()`), so no float time survives into the fight.
**Ceiling and why.** Floating-point non-associativity across compilers is the classic determinism killer for cross-platform rollback; inside one engine build it is a non-issue. Ceiling here: single-build determinism, with all math in one module so a fixed-point swap is local.
**Test.** `-- determinism`: a 24,000-tick scripted input log run three times — twice tick-by-tick, once through the *same* `TNBClock` the game uses fed 7–40 ms frame slices — with `hash_state()` (bit-exact over positions, pools, timers, queues, flags, RNG state) every 60 ticks. A different seed must diverge.
**Result.** 8/8. 400/400 hashes identical across all three runs; the log is shown to exercise both offenses, counters, flash windows, interrupts, drops, eight-counts, segment ends, evasions, blocks, clinch scrambles, sprint-steps and sensors; seed 54321 diverges.

## C2 — Geometric hit resolution replaces dodge tables — PROVEN (table) / MODEL (some cells)

**Claim.** Resolving punches by geometry — a punch is a region in the attacker's frame (reach, lateral window, height band); a defender's head is a point displaced by the active dodge — reproduces the sourced head-movement consensus as an *emergent* table, with no dodge×punch lookup anywhere in the code.
**The regions** (attacker frame, right hand; the left hand mirrors the lateral window). Distance *d* along the line of attack, lateral *l* along the attacker's right, height *h*:

| Punch | reach (m) | lateral window l | height band h |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Straight | 2.05 | [−0.18, 0.18] | [1.35, 1.80] |
| Overhand (flared) | 2.00 | [−0.25, 0.25] | [1.10, 1.85] |
| Hook | 1.90 | [−0.15, 0.50] | [1.35, 1.75] |
| Hookercut (haymaker) | 1.90 | [−0.20, 0.55] | [1.20, 1.80] |
| Uppercut | 1.75 | [−0.15, 0.15] | [0.95, 1.90] |

**Why the hook window is asymmetric.** A hook's fist runs on an arc around the punching shoulder (offset ≈ +0.2 m to the attacker's right). Where the fist has reached the target plane *y_t* is the set of laterals with |x − 0.2| ≤ √(r² − y_t²); with r ≈ 0.7 m and y_t ≈ 0.6 m that is x ∈ [−0.16, +0.56] — the region is centred on the shoulder line, not the centreline. A head displaced toward the hooking hand stays inside the arc; a head displaced the other way is past where the arc ends.
**The head displacements** (defender frame): slip ±0.35 m lateral; duck h = 1.15; lean-back +0.30 m distance; weave ±0.40 m lateral **and** h = 1.30 (the bob goes under). Neutral head h = 1.55. Body targets sit at h = 1.10 and follow a slip at only 40 %, so a slip does not move the torso out of a body shot.
**The consensus it must reproduce — what the sources actually say.** Slips are the answer to jabs and straights, inside and outside ([Slipping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slipping)). The duck "works best against straight punches" ([four head-movement techniques](https://evolve-mma.com/blog/4-head-movement-techniques-for-boxing-and-how-to-train-them/)); the uppercut is the punch a duck must fear, and dropping vertically rather than forward is what limits it ([ducking](https://www.myboxingcoach.com/body-movement-duck/), coach's reply). Bob-and-weave "moves the head both beneath and laterally of an incoming punch" ([Bob and weave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_and_weave)). The overhand "arcs the arm downwards onto the target" and "can also be employed when the opponent is bobbing or slipping" ([Overhand punch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overhand_punch)). The pull/lean answers a jab or straight and gets hard against long reach ([evolve](https://evolve-mma.com/blog/4-head-movement-techniques-for-boxing-and-how-to-train-them/)).
**The emergent table** (inside spacing 1.55 m; H = hit, · = miss; ◇ = MODEL cell, no source found either way):

| dodge \ punch | STR | FLA | HOK | HKC | UPC |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| none | H | H | H | H | H |
| slip-away (from the punching hand) | · | · | · ◇ | · ◇ | · ◇ |
| slip-into | · | · | H ◇ | H ◇ | · ◇ |
| duck | · | H | · ◇ | · ◇ | **H** |
| lean-back (inside) | H | H | H | H | · ◇ |
| weave-into | · | · ◇ | · | H ◇ | · ◇ |
| weave-away | · | · ◇ | · | · ◇ | · ◇ |
| none @ 1.80 m | H | H | H | H | · |
| lean-back @ 1.80 m | · | · | · | · | · |

Sourced rows hold: slips beat straights; the duck beats straights and eats the uppercut; the overhand comes down onto a ducked head and out-reaches a slip only at the edge; the weave goes under hooks; the lean beats the straight at range and fails inside.
**Ceiling and why.** Fight Night Champion and its descendants resolve dodges with canned animation pairs — a slip "beats" a straight because the designer tagged it so; the space between fighters is not consulted. That ceiling exists because animation-driven combat cannot afford geometry disagreeing with the animation. A shapes-first engine has no such debt: geometry decides, animation follows.
**Test.** `-- geometry`: enumerate the 7 dodges × 5 families at 1.55 m plus the two range rows, in both directions (boss→player, player→boss) and for both hands; assert every cell; assert body shots land through slips and ducks; assert that every boss read (`_correct_dodge_for`) is a valid evasion over 400 draws; assert a scheduled read is live on the impact tick for jab, hook, uppercut and haymaker startups.
**Result.** 5/5. 0 mismatches in 4 × 45 cells; 0 of 400 reads would eat the punch.

## C3 — Input buffering removes dropped inputs without adding latency — PROVEN

**Claim.** A punch command issued while the fighter is busy is queued with a 6-tick (50 ms) life and executes on the first legal tick; a command issued while free executes on the *same* tick; a command pressed during hitstop is queued, never eaten. Net added latency for a free fighter: zero.
**Why.** Every modern fighting game buffers ([input buffering](https://supersmashbros.fandom.com/wiki/Input_Buffering), [why buffers exist](https://www.wayline.io/blog/input-buffering-responsive-game-feel), [the leniency debate](https://www.wayline.io/blog/lenient-input-buffers-ruining-fighting-games)); the debate is window length, and 3–8 frames at 60 Hz is the observed band. 6 ticks at 120 Hz sits at the tight end on purpose: FNC's grammar is expressive, and a long buffer would let flicks queue into unintended strings.
**Test.** `-- smoke` stages "C3": free fighter executes same tick; issued 3 ticks before recovery ends → fires on the recovery-end tick; issued 8 ticks before → expires unexecuted; pressed during hitstop → executes on the first unfrozen tick.
**Result.** 4/4 on all 24 bosses. The hitstop case was a real bug: v5 and the first v6 core both dropped inputs during the freeze.

## C4 — Tell floors sit above human choice reaction time by a computed margin — PROVEN (design margin)

**Claim.** Every pattern tell exceeds the expected human choice-RT for its decision by a margin the compiler prints. Hick–Hyman: RT = a + b·log₂(n+1) ([Hick's law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hick%27s_law), [Proctor & Schneider 2018](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28434379/)), with a = 225 ms (simple visual RT band 200–250 ms) and b = 150 ms/bit (classic slope). A tell asks a 3-way choice (block / slip / interrupt) → n = 3 → RT ≈ 525 ms; a known pattern collapses it toward n = 1 → RT ≈ 375 ms.
**Consequence.** The 400 ms normal floor clears the known-pattern read by 25 ms and sits 125 ms *under* a cold 3-way decision — normal strings are meant to be read by pattern knowledge (Doctrine D2). The 700 ms SPECIAL floor clears the cold read by 175 ms; the 1000 ms ARMORED floor by 475 ms. The floors encode a pedagogy, and the numbers now say so.
**Ceiling.** Hick–Hyman is a population model; individual variance is large and expert compression is real. The claim is about design margins, never about any single player.
**Test.** `python3 pbn_compiler.py --margins`: per class and per authored string, tell − RT(1) and tell − RT(3); fails if any SPECIAL/ARMORED string is negative at n = 3, or (since C40) any boss's authored transformation tell.
**Result.** GREEN over 80 strings / 24 patterns; worst SPECIAL +175 ms (Ada Okafor S3), worst ARMORED +475 ms (Anders Vey P3S2).

## C5 — Hitstop and slow-time live inside the simulation — PROVEN

**Claim.** Hitstop (frozen ticks on impact) and flash-window slow-time (0.6× rate on the boss's decision timers) are simulation state, never engine clock manipulation. Determinism survives them and replays reproduce feel exactly.
**Test.** `-- smoke`: a landed hit queues freeze ticks; a frozen tick advances no clock. `-- determinism`: the log contains counters, interrupts and flash windows, and the hashes still match.
**Result.** GREEN. `Engine.time_scale` is no longer touched anywhere.

## C6 — One resolution function for both fighters — PROVEN

**Claim.** The boss's defense is a dodge *choice* (a read, with probability shaped by the adaptation tracker and by tell state), scheduled so the dodge is live on the impact tick and resolved through the same geometry as the player's. There is no separate "boss defend roll". Counter windows lower the read probability to a quarter; a flash window to 2 %; a hissatsu to zero: fear is a number applied to a real mechanic.
**Test.** `-- geometry` runs the table in both directions and checks read validity and read timing; `-- smoke` checks `read_prob()` ordering (counter < neutral < flagged).
**Result.** GREEN. The first core fired the dodge at punch startup, so slow punches always found the head back in place — the timing assertion caught it.

## C7 — Adaptation modifies defense only (Doctrine D3), mechanized — PROVEN

**Claim.** The tracker's flags raise the boss's read probability and nothing else; boss offense is the compiled pattern at all times.
**Test.** `-- smoke`: four repeated jabs flag the 2-gram; `read_prob(tier)` rises (0.30 → 0.55); the SELECT weights and every string's tell are byte-identical before and after the flag (punish cooldowns pre-armed so no punish reweight can confound it).
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses.

## C8 — The engine executes every verb the compiler emits — PROVEN (in full since C21: verbs, responses, triggers, directives, SELECT lines, META keys, tags; the IDLE lines since C25)

**Claim.** A compiled pattern is executable in full: every action verb, every punish response and every phase trigger form resolves to sim behaviour, or the smoke names what does not.
**Mechanism.** `parse_action()` is a small VM: punches (with hand, body, counter multipliers), movement (RUSH/SURGE/ADVANCE/HERD/STEP_IN/GUARD_ADVANCE/GUARD_WALL_ADVANCE/STEP_OUT/STEP_BACK/BACK_STEP/ANGLE_STEP/LATERAL_ARC/POSITION_WIN), timing (DELAY/pause), traps (COUNTER_STATE/COUNTER_ON_BLOCK — a punch into the waiting guard is answered at once), grappling (CLINCH_ATTEMPT/DRAG_ROPEWARD — hold both fighters, drag toward the nearest rope), composites (WALK_STRING/MIXED_STRING/BODY_SERIES). `_parse_response()` is the punish grammar. Sensors the hooks ask for (passive>Ns, holds_center_Ns, guard hold Ns, perfect_play_Ns) are parsed from the pattern itself.
**Test.** `-- smoke`: static coverage over all 24 patterns (`verb_coverage`, `response_coverage`, `phase_coverage`) plus live checks of COUNTER_STATE, CLINCH and the composite expansions.
**Result.** 39/39 verbs, 53/53 responses, 13/13 phase-trigger forms (the `warnings_banked==N` form arrived with the referee model in C9). The phase *directives* — the lines a phase runs once its trigger fires — execute in full since C20 (47 of 47; 21 arrived with C19's executor), and the INTERRUPT block (which strings break, the stagger, the desync) executes from C19 as well; the SELECT block's phase lines execute from C20 and its last six lines and the META keys from C21, where `pattern_coverage()` lists the whole pattern with only the IDLE lines carried; C25 executes those (49 of 52, the other 3 flags only the presentation reads), and nothing in the 24 compiled patterns is carried.

## C9 — Every hook is live: sensors fire at their threshold and never early — PROVEN

**Claim.** Each behaviour a pattern hook names is a measurable event in the sim with an explicit threshold, and the smoke proves two things per sensor: it fires on the step the threshold is met, and it has not fired on the step before (or under the contrasting behaviour). A hook whose sensor cannot be defined this way stays inert; none remain.
**Mechanisms** (threshold in parentheses; the authored intent in NP_04):

| sensor | what is measured | fires when | contrast that never fires |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `locks_to_single_tempo` (Chiamaka) | the last 6 punch intervals | all within 15 % of their median, median ≤ 1.5 s | five equal intervals; a varied cadence |
| `neutral_reset_attempt` (Deshawn) | ticks out past engagement (+0.5 m) without leading or closing | 120 consecutive ticks | the 119th tick |
| `casts_<house>_native` (Rémy) | the cast hissatsu's `house_id` in the library vs the pattern's META `house` | equal at cast | Aurora Borealis Breaker (no house) |
| `trap_stance_hands_down` (Marta) | she drops her hands after 3 s without a lead (boss-side stance, 50 % per idle selection) | a punch executes during the stance | a punch with her hands up |
| `reacts_to_fake_tell` (Tam) | phase-1 tells with nothing behind them (35 % of selections, drawn identically) | a dodge, block press or punch during a fake | ignoring the fake; phase 2 emits no fakes |
| `audio_assist_reliance` (Choir) | reaction latency to the last three real tells with the audio cue on | all three < 300 ms | the same reactions with the cue off; the second fast reaction |
| `clinch_input_spam` (Alfie) | clinch inputs in an 8 s window | the third | the second |
| `sprint_approach` (Marta, Tam) | a sprint-step's direction vs the line to the boss | toward (dot > 0.5) from beyond 1.4 m | a sprint away |
| `straight_line_retreat` (Moira) | backing displacement without an angle | 1.2 m accumulated | any step before 1.2 m; an angled retreat |
| `backstep_chain3` (Deshawn) | sprint-steps away in a 4 s window | the third | the second |
| `rope_backing` (Vane) | entering ROPES/CORNER with velocity away from the boss | on the transition | sliding along the rope line square to him (rope_touch only) |

**Clinch scramble** (NP_03 §7, completed in PAC-055). A duel of alternating stick taps on a *shrinking* cue: four cues at intervals 700 → 560 → 448 → 358 ms (×0.8 each); the tap window is 30 % of the interval, floored at the RFX window (80–160 ms), so RFX decides the final cue (RFX 50 → 14 ticks, RFX 70 → 16). Sides alternate L/R; the button path (ENTER/A) is the accessibility parity path. A tap on the wrong side spends the cue; a tap ahead of the cue locks it — mashing cannot win. Two beats win position (opponent herded 50 cm ropeward) or, holding BODY on the resolve, a reset (both toward center, +5 stamina); fewer loses it and you are dragged. Boss grabs (`CLINCH_ATTEMPT`) run the same duel; `DRAG_ROPEWARD` needs three beats. A wobbled boss (composure < 35 %) grabs on his own at 0-range, once per segment — NP_03's "AI survival behavior". Clinch inputs count toward the holding call whether or not a scramble is running.
**Referee** (NP_01 §5, both ways since PAC-055). Fouls are resolved by geometry: a body punch that still reaches a *leaning* torso is a **low blow** (the lean lifts the belt line into it); an overhand or haymaker that lands on a *ducking* or *weaving* head is **behind the head**. A foul punch does half damage and scores no EP. The ladder — warning → −5 EP → a DROP awarded to the opponent from the third on — applies to the player and the boss alike. A boss whose warnings have reached his cap *pulls* a punch that would foul (clean fighters have a cap of 0 and always pull; a lean-back therefore neutralizes a clean boss's body work and a duck his overhand); dirty archetypes take the call until META `warnings_banked=N` and also foul on the break of clinches they win. `warnings_banked==N` is a phase-trigger form. Holding without work (three grabs in 8 s) is a referee break at −2 EP.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-054 + PAC-055 stages: 35 assertions (one fires/never-early pair per sensor; fake tells present in phase 1, absent in phase 2; the scramble won by position, won by reset, lost untapped, lost on the wrong side, lost mashing; cue intervals shrinking with every window above the RFX floor and the last one on it; RFX 70 vs 50 on the final window; low blow and behind-the-head with their legal twins (head punch into a lean; uppercut into a duck; straight into a duck misses); the player-side ladder to an awarded DROP with no knockdown counted; a clean boss pulling, Alfie fouling to his cap then pulling; the survival grab once per segment and never from range; 70 % wobble movement). `-- determinism` asserts the log exercises clinches, sprints, sensors, the referee and the survival grab and still hashes identically.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (95/95 each); determinism 9/9. The soak caught one real bug in PAC-054: two bodies dragged into the same corner collapsed to one point (no facing; the camera's look_at failed) — the sim now keeps bodies 0.7 m apart and peels a double-cornered pair toward center. PAC-055's first run caught a test-side collision: the event payload key `kind` was overwritten by the event kind itself, so foul kinds were invisible to the filter — renamed, and the pass proved the fix.

## C10 — The spellbook is data: every library effect executes from its record — PROVEN

**Claim.** Each of the 87 moves in `data/hissatsu_library.v2.json` runs through one pipeline — startup cinematic → effect block → aftermath (NP_05 §5) — with a handler for every effect type (DMG, DISPLACE, STATE, BUFF, SHRED, WALL, BURN; REDACTED = authored-absent), every named state (36) and every named buff (14); the counter profile's `window_ms`, `interruptible` and negation string are the rules of engagement, not a designer's tag; every boss desperation casts by name; the Wall Gauge (§8) is the same gauge for bosses and for the player's T2 walls.
**Mechanism.** The motion is the spellbook (§4): LB + the family's punch row (HAYMAKER→hookercut, ARC→hook, PISTON→straight, RISE→uppercut) or LB+RT held 400 ms for WALL; cost is the tier's Tension, halved for the first cast of a CHARGED entrance, with T3 gated on the unlock (since C35 the unlock opens the relay's own T3s, North Star on a FULL RELAY only) and North Star scaling with the relay chain (base 1.6 + 0.8/link). The caster is locked for the cinematic and closes 0.5 m; a jab-class punch inside `window_ms` cancels an interruptible cast (50 % refund, +5 Tension and a flash window to the interrupter). At resolve the defender's answer is read from the profile: a timed WALL negates (any T1+ breaks it at cost parity); a dodge that the C2 geometry says evades the family's row chips to the profile's slip multiplier (40 %, or a full evade for PISTON); a block takes the profile's block multiplier (60 %, PISTON 70 %, "dodge-only" 100 % unless the block is perfect) plus a heavy shred; composure bypass goes through a block untouched. A defender out of reach + 0.3 m is missed. States and buffs resolve before packets, then packets in data order. The gauge (§8): only counters ×1.0, power ×0.8, body ×0.6 and hissatsu ×1.5 chip it, composure is shielded while it stands, a break is TRUE OPEN 8 s at ×1.25, reforms follow the pattern's META `gauge_reforms`, three breaks end it; HC-0's `migrates_to_next_at` carries the remainder to the next boss.
**Ceiling and why.** Data-driven supers are common; data-driven *counterplay* is not — most action games hard-code "this super is unblockable" per move. Reading the negation string as rules means a designer can author a move's whole counterplay in one line and the smoke will hold it to it.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-056 stage: 29 assertions — static coverage over all 87 moves (0 unknown handlers, 3 sealed), 24/24 desperations resolve to records (21 by name, 3 absent), family matching and tier cost, the cinematic lock, insufficient-Tension fallback, interrupt inside the window vs after it vs by a hook, full/chip/block at resolve with the shred stacking on the move's own SHRED, the PISTON profile over five packets, Marta's non-interruptible wall + the cost-parity break, the player's own wall negating a T1 cast, DISPLACE/BURN/SHRED, foot freeze, tell-jam on him and on you, counter stance, The Mean, stamina ×2, forward glide, tension on hit, two-hit echo, beats_duck_reads (satisfied by geometry: the duck eats the uppercut, C2), T3 lock + chain scaling, arm_only, Sam's gauge factors and break/open/reform/gone, HC-0's migrant wall. `-- determinism` asserts the log casts from the library on both sides, lands and interrupts, and still hashes identically.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (124/124 each); determinism 10/10. Bugs the gates caught: the boss's read-side auto-slip resolved before its wall (a walled punch was reported as slipped); buffs declared after a DMG packet in data order never applied to that packet (states/buffs now resolve first); the survival grab fired inside the cinematic of a wobbled caster; anchors' gauges shielded the generic drop tests (correct behaviour — the tests now strip the gauge for those stages and test it on its own).

## C11 — Feints: the mind game is measurable — PROVEN

**Claim.** A punch input released within 80 ms of its startup is a feint (NP_03 §8): no punch, the punch's stamina refunded, 1 stamina spent, a 150 ms recovery. The read the boss committed to the press stands — a dodge he scheduled still runs, a guard commit becomes a 400 ms flinch — and a drawn feint costs him 1 guard and 10 cm of ground. A feint never interrupts a cast. Feints are tracker tokens, and three identical feints inside 10 s make him stop reacting to that one (he cancels the read and keeps his hands) until 10 s pass.
**Why the edge is 80 ms.** The boss reads at the press (C6) and schedules his dodge with a lead of startup − dodge/2, which for every hook-class punch (≥ 260 ms startup) falls after the 80 ms decision — so a feint is decided before his body moves and the commitment is his, not the player's. A jab's read lands earlier, so a feinted jab draws less: the grammar rewards feinting the slow punches, which is the boxing of it.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-057 stage: release at 10 ticks feints, at 11 the hook goes; over 30 seeds the read is drawn (dodges run to completion, guards flinch for 400 ms), every drawn feint drains exactly 1 guard and nothing lands; the fourth identical feint in 10 s draws nothing while another row still draws and the first draws again after 10 s; a feinted jab inside a cast's window leaves the cast running; four feinted hooks flag `F_R_HOOK` in the tracker; a drawn feint herds 10 cm and fires `player_feint` / `player_feint_hook`. `-- determinism` asserts the log feints and that some draw the read.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (130/130 each); determinism 11/11 (44 feints in the log, 22 drawn).

## C12 — The corner is data and the relay is arithmetic — PROVEN

**Claim.** A segment ends into a 4 s broadcast card (NP_01 §7: EP totals, segment winner, Tension carried) and then a 45 s corner phase with a budget of two actions (NP_10 §4), where every choice — swap, special tactic, super, staff active, save the bar — is a record in `data/tactics.v1.json` whose `effect` keys are consumed by the sim on the next segment; the corner clock does not wait, and running it out sends the fighter at 80 % stamina (NP_01 §5). The relay (NP_05 §6) is integer arithmetic on the score bug: a segment that banks ≥ 25 Tension links the chain, < 10 breaks it, a lost segment with ≥ 10 keeps it; a CHARGED entrance is a 10 s surge (+10 % feet), the first cast at half cost and T3 unlocked; chain ≥ 3 is FULL RELAY (T4 at 75, the element trail on T3); a T3 cast from R2 on names the previous fighter as the afterimage (since C35 the unlock opens the relay's own T3s to whoever enters CHARGED).
**Mechanism.** `_end_segment` computes the chain and the segment winner, emits `seg_card` and enters CARD (4 s, skippable); CARD enters CORNER with `{t: 45 s, actions_left: 2}`; `corner_action({kind, id|arg})` is the whole menu — swap (reserve or any not-yet-fought slot into the next slot; first free per `swap_first_free`, then `swap_cost`), tactic (equipped, not on its one-corner cooldown, priced in Tension, effect keys staged into `pending_fx`), super (once per dual, free), staff (per-dual charges; `tension_plus` and `reveal_next` resolve on the spot, the rest are staged), save the bar (a flag). `_load_segment` moves `pending_fx` into `tactic_fx`, applies the entrance effects (stamina cap, stamina/composure plus, guard cap, no-drop / punish-suppress / tell-highlight timers, the overrun) and the fight code reads the rest where it happens: body drain, guard decay, herding, sprint cost, feint cost, counter windows, tell length, the clock (Ice the Clock runs it at 1/0.8 while you lead on EP), Tension earn, EP on clean hits (Switch-Up). Iron Resolve turns a knockdown into a wobble at 1 composure for 30 s. The chain, the surge timer, the corner state, cooldowns, charges and the staged effects are all in the state hash, so the corner is as deterministic as the fight.
**Ceiling and why.** Team-sport games with a corner phase (Inazuma's manager layer is the model) hard-code each tactic; here a tactic is a line of data and the smoke measures its effect on the next segment, so the launch library of twelve can be authored without touching the sim.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-058 stage (24 assertions) plus the dual-flow stage's card / corner / entrance / staff checks (10) — the card (4 s, its fields, the timeout into the corner on the last tick), the corner (45 s to the overrun at 80 stamina on the last tick and not the one before; two actions, the third refused; an early send at full stamina), swaps (reserve free, a second at 15 Tension, R2 becomes MARCUS; a broke second swap and a fought fighter refused), every special in the data with its effect measured on the next segment against a control (Body Doctrine 12-stamina body hook, High Guard Order half decay, Cut the Ring free sprint + doubled herd, Feint Storm free feint + 30 s hook suppression, Ice the Clock 150 clock ticks per 120 while leading and 120 when trailing, Read the Corner ×1.25 on the live boss's first tell, Open Season ×0.8 guard cap + 500 ms slip window, Second Wind 80 → 100 after an overrun, its refusal at 5 Tension, the one-corner cooldown with another tactic still going), every super (Iron Resolve holding a fighter at 1 composure for 30 s then drops resuming and once per dual; Perfect Round 10 → 20; Switch-Up 22 EP from ten jabs and a 450 ms slip window; Hostile Crowd recorded as `enemy_corner_lock`), every staff active (Raise the Roof +8 — since C28 converting 30 crowd heat, Loosen Up 110 cap, Patch Job two charges then refused, The Reveal naming the next slot, Chalk Talk 15 s of highlight carried by the first tell then gone), save the bar (the cast refused, +5 on a win), the chain over 30 / 12-lost / 26 = [1, 1, 2], an empty 4 breaking it, FULL RELAY at 3 pricing T4 at 75, and PRIYA's T3 cast in R2 naming RORY. `-- determinism` asserts the 24,000-tick log passes two segment cards and takes corner actions and still hashes identically through the jittered frame loop.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (155/155 each, 3,720 assertions); determinism 12/12; geometry 5/5; a 16,000-frame presentation soak through KO → card → corner (two actions, three refusals, the menu drawn) → tactics live → R2, and a `-- demo seg2` soak (the aid has the scripted fighter cast North Star) that spawned the T3 afterimage ghost — two casts, two ghosts per 3,000-tick run, three runs — all with zero errors. Bugs the gates caught: the event payload key `action` collided with the event `kind` the same way `foul` had (refusals were invisible to the filter); the card timed out one tick early; the scripted determinism log sent every corner instantly (corner presses are now every 40 ticks, so the log takes actions); a hook herds 0.1 m, not the 0.2 m the first Cut the Ring gate assumed (the gate now measures the control).

## C13 — The three rings are data, and the disadvantaged side is never debuffed — PROVEN

**Claim.** Every fighter carries style / element / house (NP_06); the matchup is computed once at segment load from `data/affinity.v1.json` and `data/feud_graph.v1.json` and read by the fight where each effect lives. Ring 1: the triangle's advantaged side scores +10 % EP on clean hits and reads +50 ms on every counter window; the other side is unchanged; a boxer-puncher is never advantaged. The §1.2 style packages are live for both fighters. Ring 2: the caster's element over the target's makes the hissatsu ×1.10 and 10 % cheaper; a same-element move is a ×1.30 spike; the beaten side's timed walls stand +1 s. Ring 3: a FEUD edge earns Tension ×1.25; a house native thrown by its own blood is ×1.10. The product of the hissatsu multipliers is clamped at ×1.5 (§4).
**Mechanism.** `_dress` puts the three keys and the style's package on the fighter record (composure pool and guard capacity applied there); `_matchup` emits the card. The advantage is one predicate, `_style_adv(k)`: the triangle's truth for either side, or Switch-Up's lie for the player — so the lie can never double a true edge or remove his. EP goes through `_score_ep` (a 0.2 fraction per clean hit that lands every fifth), windows through `_window` (base × trap width × the slugger's slip tax + the extras every window shares). `hiss_mult(who)` is read while the cast still knows its move and travels into every numeric packet (DMG, SHRED, BURN, DISPLACE, WALL points); the wall's seconds are extended at the WALL packet. Packages are read at the point of use: reach in `resolve_geometry` (the attacker's), the weave in `_head_world` (the dodger's), stamina at the throw, shred and ropes damage at the landing, herding on the push, the clinch windows at the scramble, the back-step and sprint in movement. The matchup, both EP fractions and the fighters' packages are in the state hash.
**Why the packages run on bosses too.** Every pattern's META already names a style; a slugger boss whose chin is not 15 % deeper would be a slugger in the commentary only. The geometry table (C2) is measured with the packages off, because the table is the bare arc geometry; the packages scale reach and the weave and are gated on their own.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-059 stage: 14 assertions — 24/24 patterns and 4/4 cards dress to ring keys with the two authored-neutral elements counted and the card firing at every load; the triangle from both sides (an out-boxer's ten clean jabs score 22 EP and read a 450 ms slip window against Yuri while his ten score 20; a swarmer's score 20 with a 400 ms window while his score 22; a boxer-puncher 20/20; the mirror and purist lines); Switch-Up as the lie (a swarmer counts as advantaged, his edge stands) and on a true edge (nothing doubled); the element multiplier as a value (1.43 / 1.0 / 1.0 / 1.0 for FROST, GALE, TIMBER and beaten GRANITE cards against Vane) and as damage (41.8 vs 29.3), the cost 31.5 vs 35, Marta's wall 6 s against GALE and 5 s against GRANITE and the player's own wall 6 s the other way; spike × native = 1.43 and 1.573 clamped to 1.5, no home bonus for a Moreau throwing Tremblay's native; the feud (12.5 for 10 against Moreau, 10 against Kowalchuk, a lineage qualifier resolving to Reyes, a DEBT edge not counting); the swarmer, out-boxer and slugger packages measured edit by edit against the boxer-puncher control on the same seed (hook cost 4.0 inside / 5.0 at range, clinch windows ×1.25, herd 0.15 m, a 1.95 m straight out of reach, guard ×0.9; a 2.2 m straight landing, sprint 3.4, back-step 1.19 m vs 0.98 m, power ×0.9, weave ×0.9; shred 7.8 vs 6.0, chin 103 vs 90, ropes ×1.1, startup 29 vs 26 ticks, slip 40 ticks); and the boss-side edits (Yuri's chin 138 vs Sam's 120 and his haymaker's ×1.3 shred, Alfie's ×0.9 guard, ×1.5 push and shorter straight, Vane's longer straight and ×0.9 haymaker). Nine earlier gates now compute their expectations with the rings (the T1 cost, the CHARGED half cost, FULL RELAY's 75, Grain Elevator's shred, Heat Advisory's burn, North Star, the s9 resolve shred, Open Season's window) rather than assuming a ring-free sim. `-- determinism` asserts matchup cards with a ring live and identical hashes.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (169/169 each, 4,056 assertions); determinism 13/13; geometry 5/5. A 16,000-frame presentation soak with the rings live ran R1 → R2 → R3 (June swapped in from the corner) with three matchup cards, two afterimages and zero errors. What the gates surfaced: the first swarmer run failed the generic uppercut check because a swarmer's uppercut reaches 1.575 m and the test stood at 1.6 (the test moved to 1.4); Alfie's foul gate assumed the post-hit spacing of a neutral herd; and the native house bonus fired on every boss desperation from the first run, because the library already names the house of every native — Heat Advisory at ×1.43 is the doc's own worked number.

## C14 — The other mind: the rival corner is data, and both teams run the same economy — PROVEN

**Claim.** Every rival club's corner is a record in `data/coaches.v1.json` (NP_10 §9): an order archetype (§2), swap aggressiveness, scouting confidence, a tactic library, a Super with its trigger, a priority for its two actions, and one tell. The enemy seals an order from four dressed fighters by the archetype (R1 fixed as the boss asked for); his corner resolves when yours opens, on scouting rather than on your choices, with two actions at your prices — the Super once per dual on its trigger, a swap judged on the rings (NP_06 §5) against the card he expects, a tactic from his library — paid from his own Tension bar. Hostile Crowd locks a corner on either side; The Reveal names his slot after his swap. Both teams earn on the NP_05 §2 table (canon: a plain clean hit pays nothing; counters 3, flash 5, perfect block or successful slip 2, interrupt 5, drop 10, segment win 5, the CENTER exchange 2, the KO trickle), with the feud's ×1.25 and each side's Perfect Round; his desperation pays its tier from his bar and is never blocked by it; 25 banked sends his next fighter out CHARGED.
**Mechanism.** `_pick_coach` keys the record by the R1 boss's club; `_build_enemy_lineup` orders the other three by pattern `meta.slot` under the archetype; `_enemy_corner` runs at `_open_corner` in the record's priority (`_coach_super` / `_coach_swap` / `_coach_tactic`), staging effect keys into `bpending_fx` exactly as the player's corner stages into `pending_fx`; `_load_segment` applies them to the boss (stamina cap/plus, composure, guard cap, `bno_drop_t`, the lock on your next corner) and the fight reads the rest where it happens (`btactic_fx`: his body drain on you, his herding, the clock while he leads, his triangle lie, his Tension multiplier). `_earn_tension_b` mirrors `_earn_tension`; the enemy bar, chain, staged effects, cooldowns, lineup, reserves, locks and trigger flags are in the state hash. The tells are decisions the rules make observable: the Academy's `counter_picks` fires on the swap it always takes; True North's `never_swaps` fires when a better card existed and he sat on it; the Grain Kings' on the Super after a FLASH DROP; Ascendant B's on the R3 tactic; Montréal's on Ice the Clock while leading; the Hollow Circuit's on the first-corner crowd.
**Why the coach acts first.** Order sealing (NP_01 §7) hides both slots; a corner that reacted to your visible choices would be reading your cards. Acting on scouting confidence keeps the information game the one NP_10 §3 describes, and lets The Reveal be honest: it shows the slot after his decision.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-060 stage: 11 assertions — every dual opens with a corner record (archetype, trigger and tell from the data's own lists, a library the boss side consumes), R1 held for all 24 and the three archetypes' sealed orders; the Academy corner (Cal swapped up for Carter against a slugger card, free, the tell fired, Switch-Up burned in corner 1, his lie scoring 22 EP for ten); True North (no swap with Ada the better card on the bench, the tell, Iron Resolve after a drop conceded holding Vey up at 1, no Super without a drop); the Grain Kings (the Super after a FLASH DROP, not after a plain one); Ascendant B (no tactic in corner 1, Body Doctrine in the corner before R3 at 30 + 5 − 10 = 25 on his bar, his body hook draining 12); Montréal (Ice the Clock + Switch-Up when leading, the tell, his clock at 150 ticks per 120 while he leads and 120 when you do); Hostile Crowd both ways (his next corner locked and spent; yours locked with 0 actions and the refusal naming the crowd); The Reveal naming the post-swap slot; his bar source by source (CENTER hit 2, off-center 0, counter 3, his read 2, the hit that dropped you 12, segment win 5, the desperation paid 35 from 40 and still cast at 0); your bar on the canonical table (2 / 0 / 3 / 5 / 2 / 2); the feud paying him 12.5, the Netmen's Perfect Round doubling his next segment, 30 banked sending his next fighter out CHARGED. `-- determinism` asserts the rival corner resolves at both corners with an action taken, his bar earning, and identical hashes.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (180/180 each, 4,320 assertions); determinism 14/14 (his bar reached 84 in the log; every hash identical); geometry 5/5; 16,000-frame soaks on Vane and Yuri with the rival corner live, zero errors. What the gates caught: the event payload key `kind` collided with the event kind a third time (`coach_action`; renamed `action`); the earlier smoke's live-boss stages left the player down and a later stage counted him out once the coach's RNG draws shifted the boss's strings (the VM stage now resets the fight state first — the smoke was leaning on a lucky RNG path); a coach whose two actions are Super + swap never reached the tactic his tell promised (priority is data now: Montréal takes tactic before swap).

## C15 — The dual's shapes are data: five formats, condition carried, the ladder to the Golden DROP — PROVEN

**Claim.** The dual's format is a record in `data/formats.v1.json` (NP_01 §2, §8): segments, dressed each side, repeat slots, condition carry, sudden death, blind. The sanctioned dual runs five segments with five slots and three reserves on both sides; the mini three with one reserve; the Iron dual five slots from three dressed (repeat slots); the Gauntlet one fighter five times; the Blind dual is sanctioned with no reveals. Slots are drawn from the dressed cards (reserves sit out, starters in order, a short roster repeats), the enemy's by his corner's archetype for any slot count. A fighter who re-enters carries the stamina, guard and composure he left with (§6) and the corner's restores land on those numbers. Segment wins are EP majority (§3.2). The dual is decided by the score, then segment wins, then counter hits, then the Golden DROP (§3.3): any dressed fighter, the pick free, condition carried, no clock, the first DROP wins. Since C42 the table holds a sixth record, the Exhibition 2v2, which is no rotation: one segment, both dressed in the ring at once.
**Mechanism.** `_build_lineup` and `_build_enemy_lineup` take the format's counts (`_standard_order` generalises the archetype to k slots: the lowest roles open, the two highest close when there are four or more to fill, one when fewer). `_end_segment` records `condition[name]` for both sides and tallies segment wins by EP; `_load_segment` restores a re-entering fighter before the corner's effects (stamina cap min, overrun min, plus values on top). `_end_dual` walks the ladder; a tie with sudden death calls `_start_r6`, which appends an R6 slot on both sides (the freshest dressed fighter by composure fraction as the default) and hands the normal card → corner → send flow the pick; `_load_segment` in R6 sets no clock and `_drop` ends the dual on the first knockdown. The player's swap and the coach's swap open to the whole roster in repeat formats and in R6 (a card takes the slot; nothing is exchanged), and the coach scores the R6 pick on condition first (×4 of the composure fraction) and the rings second. Blind refuses The Reveal and blanks the corner's slot name; the matchup card at the bell is unchanged. Format id, lineup names, conditions, segment wins, counters and the R6 flag are in the state hash.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-061 stage: 9 assertions — the five records with both rosters and orders for each (the sanctioned STANDARD order `[vane, carter, ada, sam, vey]` with three on the bench; the Iron dual's `[vane, yuri, moreau, vane, yuri]` and `[RORY, PRIYA, MARCUS, RORY, PRIYA]`; the Gauntlet's five RORYs against five); the sanctioned corner offering six swaps, a free reserve swap then a paid slot swap, five segments to a verdict with the +5 per EP win; a DROP scored while losing the exchanges giving the segment and its +5 to the other side; equal score decided on segment wins and equal segments on counters; three drawn segments into the Golden DROP with the card naming the freshest, the free pick from anyone dressed, PRIYA entering as she left R2 (40 / 30 / 30; since C28 the cutman's +15 on her stamina), no clock, his pick by condition, the first DROP ending it, and the same the other way; the Iron dual's corner before R4 offering everyone else, Patch Job and Second Wind landing on RORY's carried 20 / 40, Vane carrying too, Loosen Up raising the ceiling without topping up; the Gauntlet's refused swap and carried stamina into R2 and R3; the Blind dual's refused reveal with its charge kept and the hidden slot. `-- determinism` adds a 66,000-tick sanctioned-format log through five segments to a verdict, hashed identically twice.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (189/189 each, 4,536 assertions); determinism 15/15; geometry 5/5; 16,000-frame soaks of the sanctioned, Iron, Gauntlet and Blind formats into their second or third segment with zero errors. Canon corrections the pass made: segment wins had been decided by the drops in the segment with EP as the tiebreak; the dual had broken ties on total EP (the ladder's second and third rungs are segment wins and counter hits; the earlier register row that said "EP" was wrong). The gates surfaced one assumption of their own: the flow stage's "Tension persists" check assumed no segment win; it now pays the +5 when the segment was taken on EP.

## C16 — The card plays without you: a pure profile on the pad's own intent, deterministic, 85 % — PROVEN

**Claim.** Any segment can be delegated (NP_10 §8): the card's own profile drives the fighter through the same intent dictionary the pad produces, one per tick, from the sim's state and the sim's RNG, so a delegated segment is as deterministic and as hashed as a played one. Delegation is a corner call (per segment, for the rest of the dual, or hands on again), free, never one of the two actions; the player keeps the corners, and a delegated segment ignores the outside fight keys. AI segments earn Tension at 85 %; a held bar is held by the profile too. The style archetype sets the range and the mix (NP_06 §1.2), the card's RFX how often it reads a tell and taps a cue, its HRT how surely it rises on the count. Since C42 the Exhibition 2v2's partner plays on his profile from the bell, and the pad passes between the pairs mid-segment.
**Mechanism.** `sim/profile.gd` is static and pure: `intent(sim, ext)` keeps `ext`'s corner keys, then decides from `sim` — the count (rise on the beat, HRT-rolled), the scramble (the cue's side, RFX-rolled), a pending feint release, resting under 18 stamina, his tell (one RFX roll per tell: jab it inside the window, plan the geometric dodge for the string's first punch via `parse_action` + the C2 rules, or brace a block; guard mid-string otherwise), his cast (jab it in the window, block the resolve, raise the card's own wall), the windows (counter / flash), the hissatsu (into an open or wobbled boss, or off a full bar, never on a held bar), movement in the archetype's band with drift and rope escapes, sprint-steps, the swarmer's grab, then the offence at the archetype's cadence with the mix, body and power rates, feints, one-twos, and a rhythm change when the tracker flags. `step()` substitutes the intent when `delegated`, tallies `auto_stats`, and hashes `delegated`, `delegate_next`, `delegate_all` and the profile's memory. `_earn_tension` scales by `corner.delegated_tension_pct`.
**Why the profile lives outside the sim's rules.** It only ever produces an intent; it cannot do anything a pad cannot, so nothing about the fight's fairness depends on it, and the fight's determinism is untouched because its randomness is the sim's own stream. Its numbers are design values, not claims — the claims are the behaviours the gates measure.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-062 stage: 8 assertions — delegation as a free corner call (per segment, for the dual, hands on), the outside punch ignored at the bell and the corner keys kept; 85 % (10 → 8.5, hands-on 10), the cast into an open wobbled boss with a full bar and none on a held bar; each archetype for 30 s against a frozen Yuri (swarmer 1.12 m, 66 % hooks and uppercuts, 16 % body; out-boxer 1.91 m, 74 % straights, 10 feints; slugger 1.65 m, 32 % power, 36 % haymakers; boxer-puncher 1.65 m, no family over 36 %; 22–46 punches and 11–20 landed each); each archetype for 30 s against the live Yuri (15–18 tell reads, a string broken, 11–14 landed, spacing swarmer < slugger < out-boxer; since C25 on his own neutral game, over two 30 s fights per archetype, seeds 7 and 8, every archetype breaking a tell in one of them); the count risen on all three beats at HRT 99; all four scramble cues tapped at RFX 99 and the position won. `-- determinism` adds the mini log stripped of every fight key over a fully delegated dual: the profile lands 64, the corners still turn, every hash identical twice.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (197/197 each, 4,728 assertions); determinism 16/16; geometry 5/5; the demo now runs the profile — 16,000-frame soaks on Vane (to R3 at 6–7), Alfie (PRIYA out-boxing Carter: 35 punches, 7 feints, 6 sprint-steps, 10 jabs at tells) and Marta, the seg2 aid (two North Star casts, two ghosts) and a sanctioned dual on the profile, all zero errors. What the first runs showed: the profile took two knockdowns per 30 s from Yuri until it learned to guard mid-string when it had no plan; its one-two window was shorter than a jab's recovery.

## C17 — The snack and the meal: scraps and spars are data on the same sim, judged from its own events — PROVEN

**Claim.** The two smaller sizes of NP_02 §2 run on the one sim with no second rule set. A scrap is a bout record (`data/bouts.v1.json`) with one micro-objective from `data/scrap_objectives.v1.json`: a hard 30 s cap, no Tension meter, no corner; the objective met ends it won, a constraint broken ends it lost on the spot, the cap is judged on EP, a count-out or a third knockdown ends it either way. A spar is one 90 s segment with full EP and drops, the personal meter capped at 50, transforms (T4) locked, no corner, and lab flags (tell-highlight outlines every tell; infinite stamina is the combo lab). Every one of the 24 objectives is judged from the sim's own events by a pure evaluator — eight forms cover them: count, streak, ladder, survive, state, first blood, finisher, constraint — so an objective can only be met by the thing the objective names, and never early. Ep1's three scraps and the Priya trial (NP_12A) run from the greybox by name with their authored cards and objectives, and `end_bout` returns the sim to the dual it left.
**Mechanism.** `sim/objectives.gd` is static: `translate(sim, ev)` maps a sim event to zero or more objective events (a clean non-hissatsu hit on B → `hit`, plus `body_hit` / `counter_hit` / `center_hit` / `stepping_hit` from the hit's own flags; a player dodge that made him miss → `made_miss`; his guard breaking, a Loud or Standard interrupt, the perfect-block-streak sensor, the scramble won, a native's cast interrupted by House, a coach's shout, a tracker flag, each punch by row, a block press, a step back, a drop either way); `on_event` advances the form (counts, the flag-reset streak, the ordered ladder, the finisher judged on the last hit that landed before the drop, the constraints that fail on their first breach and win at the cap); `check` runs once per tick for the timed and state forms (survival on `bout_cap_t − clock_t`, CORNER TRAP on `trapped_b`, the corner escape tracked from the zone with the block remembered). `core.gd` hands every event to the judge from `_ev` itself, so events raised outside `step()` count too; `step()` closes the bout on met / failed. The sim gained the events the objectives needed and nothing else: `hit` on B carries its row, foul, CENTER and stepping flags (stepping = the lunge made the reach); `interrupt` carries `loud`; `guard_break`, `punch {row}`, `block_press`, `backstep`. `start_scrap` / `start_spar` / `start_ep1` build a one-segment format on the fly and restore `dual_fmt` on `end_bout`; `_earn_tension` returns in a scrap and caps at 50 in a spar; `castable` refuses T4 in a spar; `_reset_dual` drops the coach when a bout runs. An NPC card (CHALLENGER, MARCUS, JUNE, PRIYA) overrides the opponent's stats and rings and rides a stand-in pattern's strings by style. Bout kind, objective id, the judge's counters and the lab flags are in the state hash.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-063 stage: 9 assertions — the 24 objectives each with a known form, the 30 s cap, no meter (10 earned → 0), no corner, SCR-24 offered against a coached Yuri and refused against Vane; the cap judged on EP both ways; the count forms at their count and never early (SCR-01's third body shot and not the second; SCR-04 a counter and not a plain hit; SCR-21 three counters; SCR-03 the CENTER exchange and not an off-center hit; SCR-05 a guard break; SCR-13 two perfect blocks in a row; SCR-14 the scramble; SCR-15 a stepping punch from 2.15 m and not from 1.4 m; SCR-02 two slipped misses and not a whiff from range; SCR-09 the Loud interrupt and not a Standard one; SCR-17 the ladder in order and not out of it); SCR-06 survive 20 s on the tick and a drop failing it; SCR-10 the trap at 3 s and not at 2.99; SCR-12 first blood both ways; SCR-18 five hits reset by a flag; SCR-16 the corner left unblocked and not after a block; SCR-20 the House read for kowalchuk and not for tremblay; SCR-24 his shout heard live; the finishers right and wrong (SCR-07 uppercut / hook, SCR-22 body / head); the four constraints broken and holding, the held one paid at the cap on EP; the spar's 50 cap, T4 locked with T1 castable, infinite stamina through a power haymaker, every tell outlined, no corner, the 90 s end on EP; Ep1's four bouts by name with their cards (MARCUS's chin from his card on Yuri's strings, JUNE a Swarmer on Ada's, PRIYA at RFX 70 with tell-highlight) and the trial met by going the distance, `end_bout` back to the mini-dual. `-- determinism` adds a scrap (survive 20 s) on the profile against the live Yuri, run to its end and hashed identically twice.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (206/206 each, 4,944 assertions); determinism 17/17; geometry 5/5; 16,000-frame soaks of Ep1's three scraps and the Priya trial, SCR-06 against Yuri and a tell-highlight spar, all on the profile, zero errors. What the gates caught: the judge at first only saw events raised inside `step()` and missed the ones the sim raises from its own resolution paths (it now sits in `_ev`); the lab's tell-highlight was overwritten by the tactic block that follows it in `_load_segment`; the back-step tracker's initial stamp suppressed the first 20 ticks; SCR-18's test threw three identical jabs and the tracker flagged them for real — a gate correcting a test, not the sim; the spar's EP verdict and the Ascendant B corner gate both needed an EP lead once segment wins paid on EP.

## C18 — Recon is a ledger the sim carries: Pattern Intel earned by the sizes, spent on the tell, never on a read — PROVEN

**Claim.** Pattern Intel (NP_02 §3) is a ledger in the sim's state: PI 0–3 per House dialect and per named boss, handed in at setup and hashed with everything else. An opponent's dialect is his House's (the ring key), his own id when he has none, or the one a bout card names. The dialect track is earned exactly as the doc's table reads, each source on its own rungs — 3 scraps won or 1 spar fought to its end → PI 1; 6 scraps, 3 spars or 1 dual segment fought → PI 2; the boss track is PI 3 for a named boss beaten once (a dual segment won against him). The effective level against an opponent is the higher of the two. PI 1 is the commentator's call of the dialect's habit before the segment; PI 2 emphasizes his tell cue and makes the interrupt window — the tell itself (NP_04 §4) — 10 % longer against that dialect and no other; PI 3 is his Scout Book page as data. No read window changes at any level, and PI 3 is no wider than PI 2. The profile reads PI: its tell-read roll rises by a data term against a dialect it knows.
**Mechanism.** `data/pattern_intel.v1.json` holds the rungs per source, the three effects, the profile's read bonus per level and an authored habit line per dialect (a fallback names his most-weighted tell's motif). `dialect_of(name, card)` resolves the key; `pi_level` walks the rungs; `pi_boss` is the beaten flag; `pi_vs()` is the level against the current opponent (an NPC card has no boss track). `_pi_tick(source, dialect)` counts one and raises a `pi` event on a rung crossed: `_end_bout` counts a scrap won (objective, his KO, or EP at the cap) and a spar ended; `_end_segment` counts a dual segment and calls `_pi_beaten` on a segment won (EP majority or his KO), which raises `scout_book` with the page compiled from the pattern (tells, strings, phases, hooks, the desperation, the corner's tell from `coaches.v1.json`); the Golden DROP counts the same way. `_tell_mult()` multiplies both tell entries (the selected string and a forced one) by the corner's loud multiplier and, at PI ≥ 2, the data's 1.1; every `tell` event carries `cue`. `_load_segment` raises `commentary` after the matchup card at PI ≥ 1. `profile.gd` adds `pi_read_bonus()` to its read roll. `parse_pi_arg` turns `-- pi=kowalchuk:2,yuri_kowalchuk:3` into a ledger for the greybox; F12 reads the page.
**Why the ledger is state and not a save-file side effect.** The ticks land inside the sim's own end-of-bout and end-of-segment paths, so a replayed log ticks the same rungs at the same ticks, and a ledger seeded before the bell changes the hash from tick 0 — recon is part of the fight's inputs, the way a lineup is.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-064 stage: 8 assertions — the data's rungs against the table and every dialect of the 24 bosses with an authored habit, the key by House / own id / card, a fresh ledger PI 0, the seed parser; the scrap track (two won scraps vs Kowalchuk leave 0, the third is 1, the sixth is 2 and not the fifth, a seventh crosses nothing; a scrap lost on a constraint or at the cap pays nothing, one won at the cap on EP pays one; the real path — three body shots — counts one); the spar track (one to its end is 1, the third is 2, a count-out still counts) and one dual segment fought and lost → PI 2 with nobody beaten; the boss track (a segment won on EP or by KO marks Yuri beaten, his page with his 3 tells, strings, phase notes, the desperation and his corner's tell; Moira has no page; a ledger with Yuri beaten is PI 3 vs him and 0 vs Moira); PI 2's effect (Yuri's S1 72 → 79 ticks with the cue; 72 and no cue at PI 0 and PI 1; Ada's S1 48 on the same ledger; PI 3 also 79; his own selected tell at +10 %; slip / duck / perfect-block / flash windows equal at PI 3 and PI 0); the commentary at PI 1 with the authored line, none at PI 0, the cue on the line at PI 2, the fallback for an unknown dialect; the profile reading 172 / 199 / 228 of 300 forced tells at PI 0 / 2 / 3 on one seed; the ledger paid by Ep1's SCRAP 1 to the school dialect, kept through `end_bout` and `new_dual`, and two sims differing in hash by the ledger alone. `-- determinism` adds the delegated dual with a PI 2 ledger vs Kowalchuk: 31 emphasized tells, the commentary, the ledger ticking as segments end, every hash identical twice and different from the ledger-less run.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (214/214 each, 5,136 assertions); determinism 18/18; geometry 5/5; 16,000-frame soaks with seeded ledgers (`pi=kowalchuk:2`: the profile beat Yuri in R1 and earned his page; `pi=yuri_kowalchuk:3`; Ep1's SCRAP 1 three times over → PI 1 vs the school; a sanctioned dual → PI 2 vs Kowalchuk and Yuri beaten), zero errors. What the gates caught: the smoke's main sim earns PI 2 and then PI 3 against its boss inside the dual-flow stage, so the two punish/bait-hook gates that expected a bare tell duration now compute it through `_tell_mult()` — the ledger working on the real path before its own stage ran. A gap read in NP_04 §4 against the sim and left for the next pass: ARMORED strings are interruptible in the sim; the doc says they are not.

## C19 — The opponent's mind games: the INTERRUPT block is law, the phase script runs, and a rematch is a data layer over the original — PROVEN
**Claim.** A string breaks only if its pattern's INTERRUPT block tags it, only to a jab-class hit (or a hissatsu), and an ARMORED string never breaks (NP_04 §4); the block's response executes — the stagger it names, and Ada's `desync(3s)` as her reward state (she stalls the whole 3 s, her next tell is 100 ms wider). Phase directives execute where the vocabulary is the remix's (speed, tighten, add / retire, weights, damage / strength / movement percentages, desync) — 21 of the 47 authored lines; the other 26 are carried and named. A rematch remix (NP_04 §6) is a record of operators applied over a copy of the original at load — `feint(Sx)` (the tell plays; the shot comes `ms` late, or another string's hands come), `tighten` floored at the class minimums, `reweight`, `newstring`, `phase_shift`, `burn(Sx, after)` (after N interrupts of Sx he only feints it), `phase_directives`, any directive line — and the original is never touched; the remix beaten earns its own Scout Book page. The profile with a boss's page never jabs a tell that does not break.
**Mechanism.** `_interruptible(s)` reads the block (ARMORED short-circuits false); `_player_lands` breaks the string only for `src.jab or hissatsu` and a tagged string, sets the stagger from `_interrupt_block(s)` (regex over the response: `stagger(N)`, `desync(Ns)`, `tension_bonus`), counts `binterrupts[s]`, and otherwise raises `unbroken {why: armored | not_breakable | not_a_jab}` with the damage still landing; the desync sets `btimer = max(stagger, desync)` and `bdesync_widen`, consumed by the next selected tell. `apply_directive(text)` is one executor for phase scripts and remix ops (`_apply_directives` on a phase firing; `directive_coverage()` statically); `_set_tell` floors every tell by class; `retired` is a tag `_phase_ok` honours; `bmods` carries speed (recoveries and RECOVER divided), damage, body damage and movement. `set_remix(boss, id)` chooses a record; `_load_segment` duplicates the base and `_apply_remix` runs `_apply_op` per operator, raising `remix` with the tactician's notes; the selection rolls the feint variant (`rng` drawn only when 0 < pct < 1; 1.0 once burned) and the tell event carries `variant`; the TELL end goes to `HOLD` for the delay (`feint_b`) or builds the queue from the `into` string; `_force_string` never feints. `remixed_pattern(id)` and `remix_notes(id)` build the page side-effect free; `_pi_beaten` books the remix under `bosses[name].remixes[id]` and `scout_page(name, id)` carries `remix_notes`. `profile.gd` refuses the jab plan when `pi_vs() >= 3 and not _interruptible(bstring)`. `special_breakable(name)` (an interruptible desperation cast, or a Loud string tagged) gates SCR-09 / SCR-17, whose judge now counts a cast interrupt as the special. State hash adds the remix id, `bmods`, `binterrupts`, `bfeint`, the desync flags.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-065 stage: 12 assertions across the 24 (every pattern breaks somewhere; 34 strings hold, the 8 ARMORED among them and none of those breaks; Yuri on S1 alone, Echo's ARMORED S1 holding while S2 / S3 break, the Cartographer on all five; Vey 700 / Yuri 800 + tension_bonus / Ada desync 3 parsed; 'his special' true for Yuri and Baro, false for Marta) plus, on every boss, the first tagged string breaking and its first untagged twin holding (no stagger, no flash, no +5, the hit landing) and a hook in a tagged tell not breaking; Ada's stagger + desync + the widened tell + `desync 3s->2s` and Vey's 700; Yuri's phase 2 at 55 % executing `speed x1.1`, `tighten(S1, 500ms)`, `add S3` with the original untouched, Tam's `retire S1` at clock>30, Moreau's `tighten_all_tells -60ms` floored (420 / 700 / 1000) and `movement +10pct`, the 21 / 26 split with named lines on each side; `tighten(S1, 100ms)` stopping at 400 and a SPECIAL at 700, `tells -900ms` leaving an ARMORED at 1000; Yuri's rematch over a copy (feint 300 ms at 40 %, burn 3, S2 450, weights 40/40/20, four notes), the original untouched, `''` restoring it, another boss's base refused, every authored remix loading; the delay feint (tell as S1's → HOLD 36 ticks → the haymaker no earlier than 300 ms; 0 % straight; a jab in the feint's tell still breaks it); Moira's cancel (S2's tell, S1's L_HOOK first, never the overhand); burn after three and not two; Carter's S4 at 450 / weight 25 with parsing actions and S2 420, Moreau's phase 2 at composure<70 firing at 65 % where the original does not, Tam's phase 2 keeping S1, the remix beaten booking its own page (four notes) with the original's unearned; the profile with Yuri's page jabbing Coupling 0 of 300 (218 slips / blocks) against 91 at PI 0, and Freight 122. `-- determinism` adds the mini log against the remixed Yuri (10 variants told, 9 carried through, 19 hits taken in a tell that does not break), hashed identically twice and different from the original's.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (226/226 each, 5,424 assertions); determinism 19/19; geometry 5/5; margins GREEN; 16,000-frame soaks with Yuri's, Moira's and every remix live (Carter's remix beaten in a sanctioned dual, its page earned; Ada at PI 2 desyncing ten times), zero errors. What the pass corrected in the sim (the doc was right, the sim was not): every string had been breakable by any clean hit; now the block decides and only a jab does — which meant the determinism coverage log (seed 777) stopped dropping anyone and moved to seed 778, and the smoke's SCR-09 / SCR-17 gates moved from Ada (her SPECIAL is not tagged) to Baro. What the first runs showed: the profile jabbing tells that do not break and eating the string — the reason it now reads the page.

## C20 — Every phase script runs as written: the 47 authored directive lines are each a boss's own mechanic — PROVEN
**Claim.** The phase blocks of all 24 patterns execute in full (NP_04 §7, NP_04A, NP_04B): every one of the 47 directive lines is a behaviour the sim performs and a gate measures — the cadences (Ada's fixed metronome and its tightening, Chiamaka's alternating tempos), the kits (Vey's three, Vasile's by the clock with the 700 ms neutral seam and the fundamentals-only finale, the Cartographer's erased dialects), the tiers (Vane's calls hidden, Cal's tells loudened), the recognition drift, the dialogue flags, the per-string growths (a second body hook, a fifth hit), the state durations (Osei's Shell, Marta's Mirror) and Marta's cooldown, Alfie's pull, Tam's true pattern, Baro's faster corrections, Sora's nothing — with the META supplying the values the lines move from and the SELECT block's phase lines applied when the phase arrives.
**Mechanism.** `_declare_bases()` reads the compiled META at load (`metronome_ms`, `metronomes_ms`, `splice_every`, `splice_open_ms`) and applies `punish_only:` SELECT lines; `apply_directive` gained one case per line, all writing to `bmods` or the live pattern: `metronome` / `tempos` set the RECOVER cadence (no RNG draw for those bosses), `splice_every` opens the guard for `splice_open_ms` after every N-th completed string, `_tier_shift(n)` moves every tell 150 ms per tier under the class floors, `_kit_swap` retires every string not tagged for the phase, un-retires the phase's, applies `_apply_phase_reweights(bphase)`, re-dresses the boss (`_redress`: style, element and package change, composure and guard kept as fractions) and re-reads the matchup card (`matchup {swap: true}`), `dialect_swap` does the same and turns `bdialect`, `_neutral_reset` parts the fighters to 1.8 m and idles him for the seam, `fundamentals_only` retires every string that loops a punch and drops the element, `<S> state a->b` / `<S> duration +n` override the queued counter_state's ticks (`_override_states`), `<S> cooldown_after_use` keeps a string off `_select_weights()` until its tick, `select_drift` adds +25 to the string with the most reads (`breads`: tells reacted to or broken), `dirty_flirtation_stops` pulls every foul, `true_pattern_only` ends the FAKE branch, `corrections_narrated_faster` halves the punish cooldown, the dialogue lines raise `dialogue {which}`. A named phase ("finale") fires once (its number is its place in the list; it used to fire every tick its trigger held). `_select_weights()` is the one SELECT: phase-gated, retired and cooled strings out, the authored default only for strings the block never weighted, the drift on top. State hash adds the cadence index, the strings-done count, the cooldowns, the reads, the dialogue flag and the true-pattern flag.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-066 stage: 8 assertions — 47 / 0 from `directive_coverage()` and the META bases (800; 600 / 900; every 2nd, 500 ms; Ada's S3 at weight 0 and off her SELECT), the SELECT block at 9 executed / 6 carried; Ada's RECOVER 96 / 96 ticks then 78 in phase 2 and Chiamaka's 72 / 108 / 72 then 66 / 102, measured between strings; Vane 700 → 550 and 600 → 450 with S3 added, Cal 480 → 630 and 700 → 850 with the corner dialogue, his phase 3 drift lifting S2 from 35 to 60 after four reads; Alfie at two warnings pulling the next low blow with the count held and w(S1) 45 → 60; Vey at 70 % Swarmer / Gale with P1 retired, P2 at 55 / 45, a swap matchup card and his condition kept at 65 %, at 40 % Slugger / Granite; Vasile Granite at 30 s with the seam (`neutral_reset` 700, both at 1.8 m, idle for 84 ticks), Gale at 60 s, the finale at 9 % locking to F1 / F2 with no element; the Cartographer erased Berube at 70 % (S1 / S2 retired, S3 / S4 at 50 / 50, the key turned) and erased Oduya at 40 % (S5 at 100); Echo OPEN after his 2nd string and not his 1st, after the 3rd in phase 2; Carter's S2 ending in LT_R_HOOK, Deshawn's MIXED_STRING(5) parsing to five, Osei's Shell 3.0 → 4.0 s, Marta's Mirror 2.5 → 3.5 s and S1 off the SELECT for 6 s after a use; Remy's shame dialogue with w(S3) 20 → 30, Sora's line executing with strings / weights / modifiers identical, Baro's punish cooldown 240 ticks against 480 with his tells −40. `-- determinism` adds the delegated dual against Vasile: his clock kits turning twice with two seams and eight lines executed, every hash identical twice.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (234/234 each, 5,616 assertions); determinism 20/20; geometry 5/5; margins GREEN; 16,000-frame soaks on Vey (two kit turns, the dialogue), Vasile (the seam and the finale), Ada, Echo, Cal at PI 2, every remix live, Chiamaka in a sanctioned dual (two dialect swaps on the Cartographer, her desyncs), zero errors. What the gates caught: the event payload key `kind` collided with the event's own key a fourth time (`dialogue`; renamed `which`); a named phase fired every tick its trigger held (214 directive events in one log) until its number became its place; an older Tam gate had set `bphase = 2` by hand and now lets the clock trigger run the lines.

## C21 — The pattern, all of it: every authored META key, SELECT line, directive and tag executes or is presentational — PROVEN
**Claim.** Nothing in the 24 compiled patterns is carried by the sim without meaning, except the IDLE lines. The SELECT block's remaining six lines execute (an after-hit transient with its expiry, the rope-zone weight bonuses with the `zone==ROPES` tag gating the string, the clock speed lines, the patience line); every META key with a mechanic behind it does too — Alfie's herding bonus, Vey's PI transfer (the real house his kit copies lends its PI at half), Remy's extraction (his own house's PI at half), the Cartographer's PI override and half-blank page, Deshawn's no idle weight, Osei's secret at 70 % with the stumble window, Baro's Graduación gated on Examen landing, Vasile's corner intel (the kit called 2 s early from your corner), Cal's corner assist (every tell named), Reyes' target split, Vey's opening kit — and `pattern_coverage()` lists every key, line, directive and tag as executed or presentational, with only the IDLE lines named as carried.
**Mechanism.** `_declare_bases()` parses the SELECT block's conditional lines into `bselect_lines` and reads the META keys into `bmods`; `_select_weights()` applies the rope and patience conditions and the `btransient` deltas (`_on_hit_taken` sets or refreshes them from the clean-hit path) and drops strings whose zone tag does not hold (`_zone_ok`); `_bspeed()` multiplies the phase speed by the clock lines at the three cadence sites; `_apply_meta_kit` dresses the opening kit after the META dress; the walk-back adds `(herd_mult − 1) × step`; `_boss_act` rolls the body target for a head verb at `target_body_pct`; `_desperation_allowed()` reads `blanded` (counted where a boss punch lands clean); `_resolve_cast` scales the boss's hissatsu multiplier by `secret_power` and opens the guard for `stumble_ms` on the exit; `_corner_intel_tick()` raises `corner_call` once per clock phase at trigger − N s; `_tell_assist` names the string on every tell event; `_transfer_dialect()` finds the roster boss whose style and element match the kit he wears and has a House, and `pi_vs()` takes the higher of his own level and that house's, `pi_effect_scale()` returning the transfer fraction (or the extraction's half) so `_tell_mult` and `pi_read_bonus` scale while the cue and the commentary stay; `pi_override` short-circuits both and blanks the page's tells and strings. State hash adds the transients, the called phases and the landed counts. Vey's PBN gained `kit=outboxer_frost` (NP_04A §8's phase-1 kit, absent from its META) and was recompiled.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-067 stage: 7 assertions — `pattern_coverage()` over the 24 (119 META entries executed, 34 presentational, 15 SELECT lines, 47 directives, every tag; 52 IDLE lines carried and nothing else); Yuri's Freight 50 → 70 after a hit taken and 50 again after 6 s, Moira's Gaff off the SELECT at centre and at 45 on the ropes, Alfie's Sunday Best the same, Marta's Frost Line 45 → 75 after 8 s of patience; Carter's speed 1.00 → 1.12 under 30 s, Sam's 1.00 → 1.10 past the minute, Alfie's 50 cm step walking you 0.875 m against Vane's 0.500; Vey's Out-Boxer / Frost kit taking a Tremblay ledger (2 at ×1.05) and not an Okafor one, his Swarmer / Gale kit taking the Okafor ledger (PI 2, scale 0.5, the tell ×1.05, the read bonus 0.05, the cue on), his Slugger / Granite kit Kowalchuk's, his own Petrov PI at ×1.10 with scale 1.0; Remy at ×1.05 and Jean-Guy at ×1.10 on the same Moreau ledger; the Cartographer reading 0 on a PI 3 ledger with no cue and no commentary, his page half-blank with two phases; Deshawn's 18 / 18 ticks between strings; Reyes' elbow cross to the body 30 of 40 against Vane's 0 of 60; Osei's Deep Roots 33.3 at 70 % against 47.5 at full (0.70) with the stumble and 60 ticks OPEN; Baro holding Graduación at 20 % until Examen lands, then casting; Cal's tells named "Whitlock: Article12"; Vasile's corner calling Granite at 28.0 s and not 27.9, Gale at 58 s, two calls, none for Vey. `-- determinism` adds the delegated dual vs Vey with Tremblay / Okafor / Kowalchuk at PI 2 (62 cued tells, the commentary, two kit turns), every hash identical twice.
**Result.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (241/241 each, 5,784 assertions); determinism 21/21; geometry 5/5; margins GREEN (24/24 compiled, 0 errors); 16,000-frame soaks on Vey with the transfers, Osei and Baro, zero errors. What the pass found: Vey's PBN META (`style=BOXERPUNCHER element=varies`) dropped the phase-1 kit the roster doc names, so no house matched his opening kit and nothing transferred until the META said `kit=outboxer_frost`; the compiled patterns otherwise already held every value the keys needed.

## C22 — The bout that pays: the reward economy is a ledger the sim pays on its own end paths and never spends — PROVEN
**Claim.** The sim pays and never spends. A reward ledger rides beside the PI ledger — XP per fighter, Spirits, Prestige, HBP, Kizuna per pair, the toasts — handed in at setup, hashed with everything else and returned with the sim; every payment lands on one of the sim's own end paths (`seg_end`, `dual_end`, `bout_end`), so a replayed log pays the same amounts at the same ticks. XP is continuous (NP_11 §6): the fight's own events earn it as they happen (a clean hit 1, a counter 2, a flash 3, a DROP 10) and the segment's end credits them, +25 for a segment won; the dual's close pays each fighter 100 (40 lost) and the dressed reserves 50 % of the dual's XP, with 8 Spirits (5 fought) and 50 HBP for the win. A scrap pays an XP trickle (15 won, 5 lost) and, won, Prestige +1–3 by its objective's tier (tier 1 is NP_12A's "+2 Prestige"); a spar pays full XP (40, +20 won), Spirits (3, 5 won) and 10 HBP for a win; the tell-highlight lab pays half (NP_02 §2.2); houseless fighters earn +10 % (NP_07 §5). Levels follow NP_11 §2's quadratic-gentle curve (48·L², level 50 at 120,000, capped) and a crossing is a `level_up` event. Kizuna (NP_08 §5) is a pairwise meter: sparring a stablemate pays +3 a time to the +15/day soft cap, adjacent rotation slots pay +5 (+8 for the win) and bypass the cap, ALLIED houses ×1.25, levels at 10 / 25 / 50 / 90 / 150 as events; two effects fight — Corner Voices (a Lv2 partner dressed: +2 % all stats) and Adjacent Trust (a Lv3 partner in the next slot: +5 % and the relay CHARGES at 20). One `reward_toast` per bout or dual says what was paid and names the PI tick when a rung rose ("SCRAP: 17 XP, +2 Prestige, PI tick vs Kowalchuk"). Beans never come from the sim; nothing in it spends.
**Mechanism.** `data/economy.v1.json` holds the matrix's launch baselines (per-event XP, the segment / dual / scrap / spar amounts, the reserve, houseless and lab percentages, the curve, Spirits and HBP by result, Prestige base / per-tier / cap, the Kizuna sources, cap, thresholds, names and effects, the toast formats). `_ev` accumulates `seg_xp` from `hit {on: B}` (non-hissatsu, non-foul: flash / counter / clean) and `drop {who: B}` while the sim is not OVER; `_end_segment` pays `seg_xp + segment_won` through `_pay_xp(name, base, why)`, which rounds `base × _xp_mods(name)` (houseless from the card's House, the lab from `lab`), books it, adds it to `dual_xp`, raises `xp {who, xp, why, total}` and `level_up` on a crossing (`level_of` = ⌊√(xp / 48)⌋ clamped 1–50). `_end_dual` calls `_pay_dual(won)` before its verdict line: the closing to each distinct lineup fighter, the reserves' `dual_xp × 50 %`, Spirits, HBP, and `_pay_kizuna` for each adjacent pair of the lineup. `_end_bout` computes `won_bout`, ticks PI, raises `bout_end {won}` and calls `_pay_bout(won, pi_rose)`: a scrap's trickle and `prestige = min(cap, base + per_tier × tier)` plus HBP; a spar's XP, Spirits, HBP, and `_pay_kizuna(name, card.name, 3, repeatable)` when the card is a `stablemate`. `_pay_kizuna` rounds `pts × 1.25` for an ALLIED pair (`FEUDS`' ALLIED edge between the cards' Houses), caps a repeatable source at `daily_soft_cap − kizuna_day[pair]` (`new_day()` clears it), books the pair (`pair_key` sorts the names), raises `kizuna` and `kizuna_level {name}` on a threshold crossed. `_load_segment` ends with `_apply_kizuna_effects(f)`: a Lv2 partner among the dressed → +2 % on every stat, a Lv3 partner in an adjacent slot → +5 %, `kizuna_effect` raised; `_charged_threshold()` (25, −5 beside a Lv3 partner) replaces the 25 in `_end_segment`'s CHARGED test. `_toast` sets `last_toast`, appends `rewards.log` (kept to 40), raises `reward_toast {size, who, xp, prestige, spirits, hbp, kizuna, pi, won, text}` and writes the feed; the text strips ", +0 Prestige" and ", 0 HBP". `parse_kizuna_arg` turns `-- kizuna=RORY+PRIYA:50` into a ledger; F9 keeps the ledger through a rebuild. State hash adds `rewards`, `seg_xp`, `dual_xp`.
**Why the sim pays but does not spend.** Every source of currency is a fight outcome the sim already judges — a segment's verdict, a dual's, a scrap's objective, a spar's end — so the payments are functions of state the hash already covers, and a seeded ledger changes the hash from tick 0 the way a PI ledger does. The sinks (shops, scouting pulls, stat raises, training, ascension) are decisions taken between fights, on the overworld's clock, and the sim has no tick to take them on.
**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-068 stage: 7 assertions — the data loaded, a fresh ledger empty (level 1 at 0 XP), the curve (4,800 → 10, 119,999 → 49, 120,000 → 50, 400,000 → 50); XP from the fight itself (two clean hits 2, a counter 4, a DROP 15;
