## C46 — The academy's syntheses: the Forge's composer without bonds, slightly weaker per move and overwhelming in breadth — PROVEN
**Claim.** NP_07 §10's Arc 3 thesis runs on the sim. The Toronto Ascendant Academy has industrialized hybrid synthesis without bonds: fielded, its prodigies fight every enemy segment in Director Vey's corner, each carrying three syntheses — hybrids composed by the Forge's own rules (C41) from two houses' natives straight off the library, priced down. Slightly weaker per move, overwhelming in breadth. The club's answer is its forged hybrids: fewer, relayed with the partner's afterimage, each with its pair.

**The synthesis.**
- The Forge's composer on both houses' natives from the library (the SECRET excepted): no pair, no bond, no native learned. The stronger native's family and damage +0.1 over the T3 floor 2.4, a tag from each house, the cinematic by its tags — then the damage ×0.85.
- A T3 at a T3's price (50), named "Synthesis {motif}-{motif}" from the Forge's motifs, its source `synthesis:academy`, its VFX `vfx_synth`, interruptible in its 700 ms windup.
- Kowalchuk × Tremblay synthesizes to Synthesis Grain-Snow: the family and tags of the club's Grain & Snow, ×2.13 against its ×2.5. Every synthesis sits under the floor a forged hybrid starts at.
- Five prodigies, three syntheses each: fifteen, distinct, over the twelve houses with natives, each house in two or more. The club's Forge stops at eight.

**The cast.** A prodigy casts by the enemy's own rule, not the relay's: at his decision point, whenever his bar pays 50 and the player is within the family's reach (+0.3), the next synthesis in his rotation. No CHARGED, no relay, no afterimage, no duo VFX, no feud or alliance history. A jab in the windup breaks it, his bar spent.

**Mechanism.**
- **The table.** `data/academy.v1.json` and its engine copy, a new content table: the club, the coach (`ascendant_a`, Director Vey's), the synthesis rule (×0.85, three a prodigy, the name rule, the source and VFX ids, reach +0.3, the counter profile) and the five prodigies (a placeholder handle, a style, one house's dialect, three house pairs).
- **The sim** (`core.gd`):
  - `ACAD` (in the content stamp, out of the snapshot) through `setup`'s new last argument; `academy_on`;
  - `_compose_parts`, the Forge's composer split out of `compose_hybrid` (the forged hybrid unchanged: every C41 gate as it was);
  - `synthesize`, `_synth_power`, `prodigy_spec` (a generic card on the assembler, C24, drilled on one house's dialect with the house left out: no trait, no home bonus), `field_academy` (between duals only), `_synth_try` (at `_boss_step`'s IDLE and RECOVER), `academy_line`, `thesis_line`;
  - `_pick_coach` takes Director Vey's corner while the academy is fielded; `_build_enemy_lineup` dresses only the prodigies (no named bench, so Vey's swaps stay inside the academy); `_load_segment` hands a prodigy his syntheses.
- **The save** (`sim/save.gd`): the settings carry `academy`; a load fields the squad again.
- **The scene** (`main.gd`): `-- academy`; the matchup card's ACADEMY line (his syntheses) and THESIS line (the club's forged hybrid — the one live for the fighter out, else the strongest — against his best, in the numbers); the SYNTHESIS toast; the soak's summary; the help lines.
- **The drive:** `tools/drive_academy.gd`.
- `hybrids.v1.json`'s notes (both copies) point at the academy's table where they said the academy was not the sim's.

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-092 stage: 5 assertions.
1. **The academy as data.** The club, Vey's corner, ×0.85, three a prodigy; the five (ASCENDANT 1 SWARMER on Okafor's dialect, 2 OUTBOXER on Tremblay's, 3 SLUGGER on Kowalchuk's, 4 BOXERPUNCHER on Delacroix-Cardinal's, 5 BOXERPUNCHER on Petrov's), three pairs each; fifteen pairs over twelve houses, of twelve with natives, none in fewer than two. A prodigy's card: tier 3, house none, three syntheses; the squad repeats past five.
2. **The synthesis against the forged hybrid.** Synthesis Grain-Snow: HAYMAKER as Grain & Snow is, ×2.13 against ×2.5 (the composer's own reading 2.5, priced down), the same tags; T3 at 50, `synthesis:academy`, `vfx_synth`, no pair, no afterimage state, interruptible in 700 ms. The squad's fifteen, fifteen distinct against the ceiling of eight, the strongest under 2.4.
3. **The cast rule.** ASCENDANT 1 at 60 in his bar and within reach casts his first synthesis (the bar 60 → 10), the next time his second; at 40 nothing; out of reach nothing; two cast. A jab thrown clean in the windup: one `cast_interrupted`, the cast gone, his bar 10.
4. **The matchup.** YURI & MARTA Sworn with Grain & Snow forged; ASCENDANT 1, 2, 3 in Vey's corner, dressed the three, the bench empty. R1: "ACADEMY: ASCENDANT 1 carries 3 syntheses - Synthesis Tide-Heat (LB + HAYMAKER, x2.04), Synthesis Heat-Bell (LB + ARC, x2.04), Synthesis Heat-Light (LB + ARC, x2.04) - no bond, no afterimage, no history" and "THESIS: your forged Grain & Snow (MARTA & YURI) x2.5 (x3.13 relayed) against his best synthesis Synthesis Tide-Heat x2.04". Won on segments, R2 MARTA against ASCENDANT 2: "... x2.5 - relayed now, x3.13 with YURI's afterimage against his best synthesis Synthesis Bridge-Snow x2.04", her hybrid ×3.125. Refused mid-dual ("the squad is set between duals"); off between duals, Yuri's squad and the Grain Kings' corner again, no syntheses, no THESIS line.
5. **The save.** The settings name the academy; written, read, built and written again byte-equal, the built squad the academy's in Vey's corner. A DualState taken after ASCENDANT 1's first synthesis restores into a sim built otherwise on the same hash, his rotation at 1.

**Gates moved:** the content-table gate lists 28 tables, with `ACAD`.

**Determinism and the drive:**
- `-- determinism` adds the academy on the delegated log: one dual against ASCENDANT 1, 2 and 3 in Vey's corner (every swap of his and the squad at the log's end the academy's), four syntheses cast from the prodigies' bars (Bridge-Snow and Grain-Snow, twice each), none against Yuri's squad on the same log; every hash identical twice and different from it. No older figure moved: the log is line for line C45's but for the new line and the count.
- `tools/drive_academy.gd`, 7 / 7:
  - the bell: R1 YURI against ASCENDANT 1 in Vey's corner, Grain & Snow forged before it;
  - the matchup card's ACADEMY and THESIS lines;
  - his bar at 60 within reach: Synthesis Tide-Heat armed, the SYNTHESIS toast, 50 off his bar;
  - J in the windup breaks it (his read cancelled, as the smoke's `throw_clean` does);
  - won on segments to R2: MARTA against ASCENDANT 2, the THESIS line relayed now;
  - H + ; with the bar full: MARTA's Grain & Snow armed relayed;
  - it lands, and ASCENDANT 2's composure takes it.

**Result.**
- **Headless gates.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (396/396 each, 9,504 assertions); determinism 46/46; geometry 5/5. Beyond the five new gates and the content-table gate, every smoke line is as it was in C45's run but for the printed save sizes and the content stamp; the determinism and geometry logs are line for line C45's but for the academy's line and the count.
- **Compiler and tools.** Margins GREEN; the compiler's selftest 10/10, and the 24 sources recompiled byte-identical to the vault's; sim_test 14/14; the hissatsu audit GREEN.
- **Drives.** The academy drive 7/7. Also the techniques drive 10/10, the growth drive 8/8, the facilities drive 6/6, the exhibition drive 7/7, the enemy-transformation drive 7/7, the Clash drive 5/5, the Awakening, Inheritance, Manifest and Zone drives 6/6 each, the formation drive 12/12, the recruit drive 8/8, the Duo drive 6/6 and the Forge drive 7/7.
- **Soaks.** Zero script errors in all of them (the scene's seed is the clock):
  - `-- demo academy boss=1`, 16,000 frames, two runs: ASCENDANT 1–3 in Vey's corner against the default four, one and two syntheses cast (Bridge-Snow, Grain-Snow); the first stood 2–12 in R3 when it ended.
  - `-- demo academy hybrid boss=1` on the Forge's sanctioned line (YURI & MARTA Sworn, Grain & Snow forged), 60,000 frames, two runs: the five prodigies to the soak's end (Vey's swaps inside the academy), 7 and 6 syntheses against 3 and 2 Grain & Snow relayed; the first stood 7–18 in R5.
  - The first run of that soak, before the fix, ended with two named benched bosses swapped into the squad by Vey's corner. The academy now dresses only its prodigies, and the smoke's matchup gate and the determinism line check it.

## C47 — The mirror spar: the tracker's record of a fighter, fought as a pattern — PROVEN
**Claim.** NP_02 §2.2's mirror lab flag, "the adaptation tracker turned into content", runs on the sim. The boss agent runs patterns, so the copy is one: the tracker's record of how the fighter fights, assembled into the pattern the sim already executes.

**The record.** The tracker (C7) reads a fighter inside one segment and forgets him at the bell. Its record (`rewards["habits"]`, per fighter, across every dual, scrap and spar) keeps what it saw:
- the punches by row and the feints (a feinted punch once, as the feint: the tracker pops its start, and the record takes it back);
- every two- and three-token sequence thrown, and the ones the tracker read (its flag);
- the counter windows his defense opened (a slip, a duck, a lean, a weave, a perfect block) and the punch he threw into each.

**The copy.** C44's mirror card, now carrying the record:
- **Offense.** Its strings are his sequences thrown three times or more (the tracker's own count), ranked by thrown × length, one inside a string already taken skipped, as many as the copy's tier holds (2 at tier 1, 3 above). Their shares of the Style's preset are its SELECT. The Style's pieces fill what the record cannot, the SPECIAL first to go; with no record it is C44's mirror as it was.
- Every row has a verb: the boss grammar gains `L_HAY` and `L_OVERHAND` for the lead hand. A feint is a 200 ms pause where the punch was.
- **Defense.** At the bell its tracker already reads his three most-read sequences, at the tier the reads reach (capped at 3). This is C7's read and nothing else (D3), and it fades like any read when he stops throwing them.
- **His answer.** When its read makes him miss, it counters as he does: his most-thrown counter punch (×1.3), at his rate (counters ÷ windows), once he has two.
- A mirror books no Pattern Intel: the pattern is his own. Any mirror beaten meets C44's Legend trial, the lab's included.

**Mechanism.**
- **The table.** `data/bouts.v1.json` (both copies): `spar.mirror` holds the rules (the count, the tier cap, the reads seeded and their cap, the counter's minimum and weight, the row verbs, the feint's pause, the label); `lab_flags.mirror` no longer says "not in the greybox".
- **The sim** (`core.gd`):
  - the record: `_habit_track` (from `_track`, on its own window `htokens`), `_habit_read` (at the tracker's flag), `_habit_chance` (at the player's evade and perfect block), `_habit_counter` (a punch into a window), `habits_of`, `seed_habits`;
  - the copy: `habit_copy`, `_seq_words`; `mirror_card` carries `habits`, `mirror_reads`, `mirror_counter`, `mirror_of`, `learned_from`; `spec_from_card` passes the habits on;
  - the bout: `start_spar`'s `mirror` flag (the fighter's copy, "MIRROR SPAR - RORY"), `_mirror_bell` at `_load_segment`, `_mirror_counter` at the player's whiff, `_end_bout` without the PI tick;
  - the lines: `mirror_line`, `habits_line`; the aid: `mirror_aid` and the OVER input `mirror` (a mirror spar for the next dressed card with a record, then back to the dual);
  - `PUNCH_VERBS` gains `L_HAY` and `L_OVERHAND`.
- **The assembler** (`sim/assembler.gd`): a spec's habits take the first string slots (normal class, on the dialect's channel, at the tier's loudness) and share the preset's weights; the pieces fill the rest in their drawn order. A spec without habits draws exactly as before.
- **The save:** the record rides the reward ledger; no schema change.
- **The scene** (`main.gd`): DUAL OVER's HABITS line for the focused card, the matchup card's MIRROR line, THE MIRROR and YOUR COUNTER toasts, `[M]` after the mirror back to the dual, `-- habits=`, `-- demo mirror`, the help lines and the soak's summary.
- **The drive:** `tools/drive_mirror.gd`.

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-093 stage: 5 assertions.
1. **The record.**
   - RORY throws JAB, JAB, R_STR three times, a feinted L_HOOK and, into a slip's window, R_HOOK: 10 punches, 1 feint, the rows {L_STRAIGHT 6, R_STRAIGHT 3, R_HOOK 1}.
   - JAB > JAB > R_STR thrown 3 times and read; the sequences through the feint as the feint, the start taken back (the tracker's too).
   - One window, one counter (R_HOOK).
   - At the next bell the tracker's 11 tokens are cleared, the record kept; PRIYA's punch goes under her name.
2. **The copy** from a seeded record (JAB > JAB > R_STR ×8, L_HOOK > R_HOOK ×5, JAB > JAB ×5, R_UPC > R_HOOK ×2, R_HOOK into 4 of 6 windows):
   - Its strings: S1 "HABIT JAB > JAB > R_STR" and S2 "HABIT L_HOOK > R_HOOK", normal, 600 ms on the gym's channel. JAB > JAB (inside S1) and R_UPC > R_HOOK (under the count) are not strings.
   - SELECT 71 / 29; the Style's punish on S1; both strings interruptible (tier 1).
   - Reads [JAB > JAB > R_STR, L_HOOK > R_HOOK, JAB > JAB] at 3; the counter R_HOOK at 0.67.
   - Byte-equal in another sim. Another record gives "HABIT L_HOOK > R_HOOK" and "HABIT feint JAB > L_HAY > L_OVERHAND" (a pause, L_HOOKERCUT, L_FLARED); at Circuit (tier 2), a third, "HABIT R_UPC > L_HOOK".
   - No record: C44's card and the Style's pieces. `pattern_coverage` carries nothing.
3. **The lab's flag and the bell.**
   - At The Ring's T0 the flag is refused and the spar is against Yuri.
   - At T1: RORY against RORY'S MIRROR, "MIRROR SPAR - RORY". Its tracker opens with both sequences at 3, the counter armed.
   - "MIRROR: RORY'S MIRROR learned from 34 punches - throws HABIT JAB > JAB > R_STR (71%), HABIT L_HOOK > R_HOOK (29%); reads JAB > JAB > R_STR (3), L_HOOK > R_HOOK (3) from the bell; counters with your R_HOOK (67%)".
4. **His read and his answer.**
   - With JAB > JAB > R_STR thrown, the tracker is at 3 and his read is 1.05, against 0.3 on an unread sequence.
   - His strings and SELECT stay byte-identical (D3).
   - Unrepeated, the reads are gone after 24 s.
   - His read makes a jab miss: at the rate 1 he answers with R_HOOK at ×1.3; at 0, never.
5. **The end, the save and the aid.**
   - Won: trials `spar_won` and `mirror`, no PI (a plain spar ticks it), and the record grown by the spar's punch (35).
   - The save is written back byte-equal with the same record. A DualState taken after the bell restores into a sim built otherwise on the same hash, with the copy's strings and its reads.
   - The aid: RORY'S MIRROR, then back to the dual (FIGHT), then PRIYA'S MIRROR.
   - A mirror spar with an objective is judged as any spar's (C17): SEGMENT_END met at the segment's end.

**Gates moved:** none.

**Determinism and the drive:**
- `-- determinism` adds the mirror on the delegated log: the dual against Yuri's squad records RORY, PRIYA and JUNE (RORY 70 punches). Between duals the aid spars RORY's mirror ("HABIT JAB > JAB", "HABIT R_HOOK > JAB"), his counter is thrown back twice, and the aid goes back. Every hash is identical twice and differs from the same log without the aid.
- One older figure moved: the save-and-load line reads 239 KB (was 218), because the record is in the SaveGame's ledger and the DualState's. Every other line is C46's.
- `tools/drive_mirror.gd`, 8 / 8:
  - the dual won: DUAL OVER's HABITS line;
  - [M]: RORY against RORY'S MIRROR and the MIRROR line;
  - its strings and the reads at 3;
  - J, J, then K: read at 3, slipped, a miss;
  - his R_HOOK thrown back, and the YOUR COUNTER toast;
  - the spar won on EP: the trial, no PI, the record grown by three;
  - [M]: back to the dual.

**Result.**
- **Headless gates.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (401/401 each, 9,624 assertions); determinism 47/47; geometry 5/5. Beyond the five new gates every smoke line is as it was in C46's run but for the printed save sizes and the content stamp; the determinism log is C46's but for the mirror's line, the save's size and the count; geometry is line for line C46's.
- **Compiler and tools.** Margins GREEN; the compiler's selftest 10/10, and the 24 sources recompiled byte-identical to the vault's; sim_test 14/14; the hissatsu audit GREEN.

## C48 — Accessories and gear: cosmetic-forward pieces in the rarity's slots, each passive its kind's and under the board's — PROVEN
**Claim.** NP_11 §5 runs on the sim's ledgers. Gear is cosmetic-forward — gloves, wraps, robes, mouthguards — each piece a look and at most one small passive. A fighter wears one piece of each kind in the accessory slots his rarity opens (C44: 1 / 2 / 3 / 3), set between duals. The club keeps one locker, and the sources are NP_11 §5's four plus the signature pieces.

**The pieces.**
- **The passive is its kind's,** on the board passive's own hook, never over the table's cap (3 %):
  - gloves: the jab (Snap's hook, +30 % on the board);
  - wraps: the guard lost holding (Tight Guard's, −20 %);
  - a mouthguard: the wobble (Iron Jaw's, −30 %);
  - a robe: the personal Tension contribution (Sparkplug's, +15 %).

  One piece a kind means a full kit never puts more than the cap on any key, so the board's passive always outweighs it (NP_11 §5: "gear never outweighs boards"). Some pieces are cosmetic only.
- **The look:** each piece has a colour and a material; the greybox's figure wears it (gloves at the hands, wraps behind them, the mouthguard at the jaw, the robe at the walk-out).

**The sources.**
- **The HBP shop,** on the technique shop's three shelves (C45): t1 as found (60–80 HBP), t2 with the Regionals (250–300), t3 with the Provincials (500). A piece bought twice is two copies.
- **The circuit's prizes,** with their flags: the Regionals Robe (`story:regionals`), the Provincials Gloves (`story:provincials_semifinal`), the Nationals Guard (`story:nationals`).
- **A blessed house's gift** (C31): its colours, the piece by the house's style bias (NP_07 §8): an Out-Boxer house's gloves, a Swarmer house's wraps, a Slugger house's mouthguard, a Boxer-Puncher house's robe, at 2 %.
- **The festival's prizes:** an Exhibition 2v2 won on the boardwalk (C42) pays the next of three.
- **Signature gear,** a named character's identity piece, his alone: Yuri's Grain-Sack Wraps (NP_11 §5), and Efua's mother's wraps with her questline (NP_07A Q-OSEI). Nobody else wears it and it is never sold.

The story's pieces are read off the ledgers as they stand — flags, blessings, the club's cards — and never stored, so a load holds the same locker. What the club buys or wins at the festival is the ledger's.

**Mechanism.**
- **The table.** `data/gear.v1.json` and its engine copy, a new content table:
  - the cap; the four kinds (key, direction, board passive);
  - the shop's shelves, the festival's order, the house gift's rule and the houses' styles;
  - eighteen pieces (name, kind, percent, source, shelf and price, or flag, or owner; colour and look).
- **The sim** (`core.gd`):
  - `GEAR` (in the content stamp, out of the snapshot) through `setup`'s new last argument;
  - the locker: `gear_item` (a house's gift by rule), `gear_text`, `_gear_derived`, `gear_owned`, `gear_from`, `gear_free`, `_gear_sync` and `gear_seen` (the toasts, at setup and every new dual), `gear_festival`;
  - the card: `gear_worn` (cut to his slots at the bell), `gear_fx`, `_gear_mult` (at the jab's damage, the guard's decay, the wobble's recovery and the Tension earned), `gear_why`, `_gear_slots_why`, `gear_best`, `wear`, `take_off`;
  - the shop: `gear_shelf_open`, `gear_buy_why`, `gear_buy_best`, `buy_gear`;
  - `gear_lines`, `gear_line`, `gear_aid` and the OVER input `gear`, `seed_gear`; `P["gear"]` and `P["gear_fx"]` at `_load_segment`.
- **The exhibition** (`sim/exhibition.gd`): the festival's prize at `_end` for a win on the boardwalk.
- **The save** (`sim/save.gd`): each Fighter's `gear` — NP_11 §9's `[item_id, ...3]`, a view of the ledger's locker.
- **The scene** (`main.gd`):
  - DUAL OVER's GEAR lines, `\` (the shop's pick), `,` (the best free piece on) and Shift+`,` (the last off);
  - the GEAR toasts, the matchup card's GEAR line and the figure dressed in the pieces' colours;
  - `-- gear=`, `-- demo gear`, the help lines and the soak's summary.
- **The drive:** `tools/drive_gear.gd`.

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-094 stage: 5 assertions.
1. **The table.** The cap 3 %.
   - The pieces by source: shop 10, prize 3, festival 3, signature 2; none over the cap.
   - Each kind's strongest against its board passive: gloves 3 against Snap's 30, wraps 3 against Tight Guard's 20, mouthguard 3 against Iron Jaw's 30, robe 2 against Sparkplug's 15.
   - Twelve houses' gifts by rule: "Tremblay colours - gloves (jab damage +2%)", Okafor's wraps, Kowalchuk's mouthguard, Tagaq's robe.

**Gates moved:** the content-table gate lists 29 tables, with `GEAR`.

**Determinism and the drive:**

## C49 — The obsolescence audit: the earliest three built for the endgame on live systems, fought, and the failing system named — PROVEN (the gate runs; on this build its verdict is RED)
**Claim.** NP_11 §10's recurring design gate runs headless (`-- audit`). The three earliest-recruitable fighters are built for the current endgame with live systems only, then fought against its bosses. A fighter who falls short names the failing system, with the numbers, not himself.

**The fighters and the endgame.**
- **The earliest three, in NP_12 §4's order:** PRIYA (beat 1, Ep1: "Priya recruited"), DEX (beat 3, "Building five"), YURI (beat 4, the Regionals pool's Grain Kings).
- **The endgame:** the arc-3 patterns (Alfie Crane, Chiamaka Okafor, DeShawn Vaughn, Ioan Vasile, Itzel Reyes-Ortega, Sora Kannagi, Yordanis Baro) at level 35 (NP_11 §2: "a story-pace player hits ~35 by Arc 3 start").
- **The reference:** the recruit table's arc 2–3 recruits (Cassidy, Remy, Jean-Guy, the Choir), built the same way.

**The build, live systems only** — each step a verb that refuses when its rule is unmet:
- the endgame's XP on his own curve (the no-name's +10 %: PRIYA reaches 36);
- the three trials marked by their own code paths (a spar won, a transform weathered, the mirror beaten), and each ascension paid in Spirits;
- the board by the aid's picks until the points run out;
- the raises on his lowest stat until the jar runs out;
- a kit from the live sources (the shop's open shelves, the masters' open lessons), chosen so every move answers a motion;
- the gear the locker and the shop fit.

**Viability.** NP_11 §10's three:
- **tier 4;**
- **a competitive board:** every Board Point but one spent, three passives or more;
- **a coherent technique kit:** every slot the level opens filled and every carried move answering a motion.

And holding the endgame: half his segments or more won against its bosses, in spars on the profile (two seeds each).

**The kit's rule made explicit.** `kit_unreachable` states C45's cast order. LB + a motion casts the card's own move on its family, else the kit's first of that family the bar can pay. So a move answers at some bar only when every move ahead of it on its family costs more.

**Mechanism.**
- **The table.** `data/audit.v1.json` and its engine copy (read by the audit, not the sim):
  - the earliest three with their NP_12 sources, the reference recruits;
  - the endgame's arc and level, the budget (95 Spirits, 100 Beans, 3,000 HBP, the circuit's flags by Arc 3);
  - the criteria (points left, passives, the 50 % bar, the seeds, a 10 % stat gap);
  - the systems each shortfall names.
- **The audit** (`sim/audit.gd`, static): `mk`, `endgame_bosses`, `card_of`, `learnable_families`, `build_kit`, `build`, `fight`, `run`, `lines`.
  - **The naming:** a shortfall on tier 4 names rarity; on the board, the ability board; on the kit, techniques. In the endgame it names growth when his built total is over 10 % under the reference's, else the fight itself (tuning, PAC-052's lane).
- **The sim** (`core.gd`): `kit_unreachable`; `load_content` reads the table.
- **The tests:** `sim/tests.gd`'s `audit()`, reached by `-- audit` (`main.gd`). It exits 0 when all three are viable, else 1 naming the failing systems.

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-095 stage: 5 assertions.
1. **The audit as data.**
   - PRIYA, DEX, YURI (a Millbrook card, then two arc-1 recruits); the reference's arcs 2, 2, 2, 3.
   - The seven arc-3 bosses, level 35, the budget, the 50 % bar; the three cards (Out-Boxer houseless, Slugger MacIsaac, Slugger Kowalchuk).
2. **A build on live systems only.**
   - YURI: 58,800 XP, L35, tier 4, 69 of 70 points, 7 passives, 14 raises (the jar empty), a kit of Chinook Rush, Landslide, Snowblind and Roadwork with every move answering (3 of 4 slots and the T0), 3 of 3 gear.
   - PRIYA: 64,680 XP, L36, tier 4, 72 of 72 points, her own Marginalia in the T0.
   - Each shortfall is the kit alone. On 50 Spirits the build stops at tier 3: "Legend costs 60 Spirits (the ledger holds 25)".
3. **The kit's rule and the builder.**
   - Updraft behind YURI's Grain Elevator never answers; Snowblind behind PRIYA's Marginalia never answers.
   - [Snowblind, First Snap] both answer; [First Snap, Snowblind]: Snowblind does not.
   - The builder's kits hold no such move, each T1 on a family of its own.
4. **The fight.** YURI against Alfie Crane (seed 11): 6–0 on drops, EP 26–2, won, in 3,064 ticks. The same twice; another seed another state.
5. **The verdict names the system** (one boss, one seed, one reference):
   - a bar nobody reaches puts PRIYA's shortfall on growth (her total 17 % under the reference's);
   - with the gap allowed, on the fight itself;
   - a purse short of the Legend, on rarity;
   - the kit's three families, on techniques;
   - at level 25 (three slots) with the bar at 0, the machinery says VIABLE.

   The report's lines.

**Gates moved:** none.

**Determinism and the audit:**
- `-- determinism` adds the audit reduced: DEX built (L35, tier 4, total 472, the kit and gear) and fought against Alfie Crane and Ioan Vasile, identical twice to the last number and spar hash, a seed apart different. Every older line is C48's.
- **`-- audit` on this build: RED, 0 of 3 viable.**
  - **Techniques, all three:** at level 35 the kit opens four technique slots. The live sources teach four motion families (RISE, ARC, PISTON, HAYMAKER), every learnable T1 at the same price (35), and the card's own move holds one family (PRIYA's T0 Marginalia shadows PISTON's T1s). So three families are left for four slots, and no fourth move ever answers. NP_05 §4's WALL family is the T2 walls, not the club's to learn (C45). The reference recruits share it.
  - **Growth, PRIYA:** 3 of 14 segments against the arc-3 bosses (21 %, the bar 50 %). PWR 50 against the late recruits' 80, CHN 53 against 79; her total 457, 17 % under their 551. The Out-Boxer board carries PWR +5 and CHN +12 in all, and the raises price by count.
  - **DEX and YURI hold the endgame:** 10 of 14 (71 %) and 14 of 14. The reference: 11–13 of 14.

  The fixes are PAC-096's, the audit their acceptance test (NP_11 §10: "the failing system — not the fighter — gets fixed"). Since C50 both are made and the verdict is GREEN.

**Result.**
- **Headless gates.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (411/411 each, 9,864 assertions); determinism 49/49; geometry 5/5. Beyond the five new gates, every smoke line is as it was in C48's run, byte for byte, the save sizes and the content stamp included. The determinism log is C48's but for the audit's line and the count; geometry is line for line C48's.
- **The audit.** `-- audit` RED (exit 1), 0 of 3 viable, the failing systems techniques and growth, with the numbers above. Two runs are identical line for line.
- **Compiler and tools.** Margins GREEN; the compiler's selftest 10/10, and the 24 sources recompiled byte-identical to the vault's; sim_test 14/14; the hissatsu audit GREEN.
- **Drives.** Nothing on the scene changed, so there is no new drive. All 17 are green: gear 7/7, mirror 8/8, academy 7/7, techniques 10/10, growth 8/8, facilities 6/6, exhibition 7/7, the enemy-transformation drive 7/7, Clash 5/5, the Awakening, Inheritance, Manifest and Zone drives 6/6 each, formation 12/12, recruit 8/8, Duo 6/6, Forge 7/7.
- **Soaks.** Zero script errors in all of them (the scene's seed is the clock):
  - `-- demo gear boss=1` (60,000 frames): the robe from the locker and three Fitted Guards bought and worn, as in C48's run;
  - `-- demo mirror boss=1 facility=ring:1` (60,000 frames): 5 counters thrown back;
  - `-- demo growth` (29 steps);
  - `-- demo hybrid`.

## C50 — What the audit named, fixed: stat totals by rarity and the defensive board's walls — PROVEN (`-- audit` GREEN, 3 of 3 viable)
**Claim.** C49's RED named two systems, and both fixes are canon lines the build had not read yet:
- NP_11 §1's "Stat totals by rarity keep early recruits viable";
- NP_05 §3's T2 unlock, "Anchor-class fighters + defensive boards".

With them the audit says GREEN. The audit itself is unchanged: `sim/audit.gd` is untouched, and so are its criteria and budget.

**The fixes.**
- **Growth: stat totals by rarity (NP_11 §1).** A card under his tier's total is lifted to it. The totals are Circuit 420, National and Legend 490 (the late recruits' card, 70 a stat, where the catch-up ends).
  - His own stats are scaled up in proportion, so he keeps his shape: PRIYA's RFX stays her best.
  - Each stat stops at the tier's cap; a stat of his own above it stands. What the cap turns away goes to his lowest stat.
  - The board and the raises add on top, so every node and raise still counts.
  - A card at or over the total never moves. Nothing is stored: the lift is read off his rarity each time.
- **Techniques: the defensive board (NP_05 §3).** Every layout's Spirit lobe gains the Anchor Wall (3 Board Points, beside Wallflower).
  - Once it is taken, the board teaches the three board walls, free; the node is the price. They are Shield Wall (NP_05 §10's "Anchor board" row), Windbreak and Palisade, each T2 WALL at 25.
  - A T2 in a technique slot needs the node from any source (the lesson, a tape, the shop, a re-teach). A respec takes the wall out of the kit; it stays learned.
  - WALL is a fifth motion family (NP_05 §4: RT + LB held 400 ms). So the fourth slot at level 35 holds a move that answers.
- **The aid.** Its board picks seek the Anchor Wall like a passive only when his technique slots outnumber the motion families his own move leaves free. That happens at level 35, not before. Below it, the picks match the board without the wall, node for node.

**Mechanism.**
- **The tables** (both copies):
  - `growth.v1.json`: `rarity.totals`, the Anchor Wall (`s15`, kind `wall`) on each layout (61 nodes), `costs.wall`;
  - `techniques.v1.json`: `t2_board`, the masters' `BOARD` (kind `board`); `board:anchor` is out of `not_learnable`.
- **The sim** (`core.gd`):
  - `rarity_total` and `rarity_base` (under `grown_stats`);
  - `wall_open`, `wall_wanted` (in `node_best`), `_wall_label`, `_t2_why` (in `_learn_why`, `kit_moves` and `kit_why`);
  - `master_here`'s board kind and `_gate_why`'s node gate;
  - `ascend`'s event and line carry the total, and so does `main.gd`'s ASCENDED toast.
- **The tests** (`tests.gd`): 5 smoke gates and 1 determinism gate. C49's gates and one growth-drive check moved (below).

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-096 stage: 5 assertions.
1. **The fixes as data.** The totals [420, 490, 490]; the Anchor Wall on all four layouts, linked to Wallflower and its neighbour; the board's three lessons behind it; the walls a slot's T2 WALL at 25; `board:anchor` open.
2. **Stat totals by rarity.**
   - PRIYA totals 370 / 420 / 490 / 490 from Club to Legend, her shape kept at each. RFX is 90 at National (the cap) and 93 at Legend.
   - SAM (475) stands at Circuit and lifts to 490 at National. RORY's own HRT 99 stands over Circuit's cap, his total 420.
   - CASSIDY (490) is never moved. A raise counts on top (493).
   - With the totals taken out, PRIYA's Legend is 370 again.
3. **The defensive board.**
   - At level 35 a T2 wall is refused without the node: the lesson, any source, and the node itself, out of reach.
   - The aid's picks walk to the Anchor Wall in 11 nodes, and the board's three lessons open.
   - Windbreak is learned (`master:BOARD`) into the kit, answering. RORY's re-teach is refused.
   - A respec takes Palisade out of the kit, still learned.
   - At the bell, LB + guard held 400 ms raises Windbreak for 5 s: its 3, +1 for Wallflower, +1 as GALE against Yuri's GRANITE.
4. **The aid and the builder.**
   - Wanted at level 25: no; at 35: yes (PRIYA and RORY).
   - RORY's 25 picks at level 20 are identical with and without the wall in the layout.
   - The audit's PRIYA carries Palisade, Timberfall, Chinook Rush and Landslide, with no shortfall.
   - With the board's walls taken out, 3 of 4 slots, and the kit is named.
5. **The fight the fixes turn.**
   - PRIYA (total 547) beats Chiamaka Okafor 6–1 on seed 11. Built with the totals taken out (451), she loses 0–5.
   - The reduced verdict: VIABLE, and growth without the totals.

**Gates moved — flag for @heat.**
- **The ability boards:** 61 nodes (was 60), the Spirit lobe 15 (was 14), RORY's board 63 (was 62).
- **The save's Fighter.board:** RORY at Circuit walks out on PWR 59 (was 52). Circuit's 420 lifts his own 384.
- **C49's gates:**
  - a build now reads 4 of 4 slots and no shortfall (was 3 of 4, the kit named);
  - the builder's kits are 5 and 4 steps (were 4 and 3);
  - the verdict's cases now name each system with its fix taken out: the bar alone names the fight itself, the totals out name growth, 50 Spirits names rarity, the walls out name techniques, and the bar at 0 is VIABLE.
- **The growth drive:** X's toast reads "a 420 stat total at least". F5's RORY walks out on PWR 55 (Circuit's 52 +2 +1; was 45 +2 +1 = 48).
- **Determinism:** C49's reduced line moved. DEX totals 555 with Palisade in his kit; Ioan is 6–0 (was 8–0). No other line moved.

**Result.**
- **Headless gates.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (416/416 each, 9,984 assertions); determinism 50/50; geometry 5/5.
  - Beyond the five new gates and the five moved (above), every smoke line is as it was in C49's run but for the content stamp (the tables changed) and the hash of C49's fight (YURI's build moved; its score and EP stand).
  - The determinism log is C49's but for the reduced audit's line, the fixes' line and the count. Geometry is line for line C49's.
- **The audit.** `-- audit` GREEN (exit 0), 3 of 3 viable:
  - PRIYA 11 of 14 (79 %), her total 547 against the references' 548–551;
  - DEX 14 of 14 and YURI 14 of 14;
  - every kit 4 of 4 slots and the T0, every move answering;
  - the references 12–13 of 14.
- **Compiler and tools.** Margins GREEN; the compiler's selftest 10/10, and the 24 sources recompiled byte-identical to the vault's; sim_test 14/14; the hissatsu audit GREEN.
- **Drives.** All 17 are green: growth 8/8 (its two moved checks), gear 7/7, mirror 8/8, academy 7/7, techniques 10/10, facilities 6/6, exhibition 7/7, the enemy-transformation drive 7/7, Clash 5/5, the Awakening, Inheritance, Manifest and Zone drives 6/6 each, formation 12/12, recruit 8/8, Duo 6/6, Forge 7/7.
- **Soaks.** Zero script errors in all of them (the scene's seed is the clock):
  - `-- demo growth` (29 steps: 2 ascensions, 19 nodes, 8 raises);
  - `-- demo gear boss=1` (60,000 frames): the robe and three Fitted Guards worn;
  - `-- demo mirror boss=1 facility=ring:1` (60,000 frames): one dual and one mirror spar this run, no counter thrown back;
  - `-- demo hybrid`.

## C51 — The faucet/sink audit: a story-pace playthrough to Arc 3 paid by the sim's own paths, against the sinks priced from the live tables — PROVEN (the gate runs; on this build its verdict is RED)
**Claim.** NP_11 §6's per-milestone faucet/sink audit runs headless (`-- faucets`): "target — a story-pace player affords 70% of desired sinks". It pays a story-pace playthrough to Arc 3's start through the sim's own reward paths and prices the sinks that player wants from the live tables. Each currency that misses is named, with its numbers. The audit also checks the obsolescence audit's own budget and level against what the faucet pays.

**The playthrough** (`data/faucets.v1.json`, first drafts for the economy lane):
- **Length.** Arc 1's 24 episodes (NP_12 §8) and Arc 2 at Arc 1's length (its beats are loose by intent), one story dual an episode.
- **Pace.** NP_02 §5's cadence (one story dual per 60–90 min, read as 75) in 45-minute sessions: 80 sessions. Each session holds NP_02 §5's shape at its midpoints: 5 scraps, 1.5 spars, 1 training slot.
- **Three phases:**
  - Arc 1 to Building five: 6 mini-duals, the Survival Dual lost;
  - the Regionals to the Nationals: 18 sanctioned duals, the final lost;
  - Arc 2: 24 blind duals, 2 lost, half of them hosted.

  Each phase runs with the story's flags and the gym at the tiers they open.
- **Results given:**
  - nine scraps in ten won, seven spars in ten;
  - a won dual carrying three segments in five, a lost one two;
  - the minigames at the median grade;
  - the captain fighting the overworld and opening every dual.

**Mechanism.**
- **The pay.** Every fight goes through the sim's own path:
  - a dual's segments through `pay_segment` (now the one place a segment's XP is paid) and the dual through `_pay_dual`;
  - a scrap or a spar through `_pay_bout`;
  - a slot through `train`.

  One sim per phase, the ledger carried between them. Nothing is spent.
- **A segment's own XP** is measured, not assumed. `event_xp` is the sim's one rule, now read by the segment and the audit alike. The obsolescence audit's builds fight its endgame bosses, and the medians of the won and the lost are taken (a side the sample never saw takes the other's, and says so).
- **The sinks.**
  - The squad of eight: each built as the obsolescence audit builds, priced off its ledger — the ascensions climbed, the raises bought at their prices, the shop's kit at its shelf, the shop's gear. Budget less what is left would undercount by what its trials' spars pay.
  - Every facility track to T3, and one Forge.
  - Prestige: none live.
- **The verdict.**
  - Each share against 70 % ± 15.
  - Each session's pay against NP_11 §6's typical/session.
  - The protagonist's level against NP_11 §2's ~35 ± 2.
  - The obsolescence budget: its three fighters funded, at its level.
- **Where it lives.** `sim/faucets.gd` (static); `core.gd`'s `event_xp`, `pay_segment` and the table in `load_content`; `tests.gd`'s `faucets()`, reached by `-- faucets` (`main.gd`), exit 0 GREEN, else 1.

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-097 stage: 5 assertions.
1. **The audit as data:** 70 % ± 15, 75 / 45, the session 5 / 1.5 / 1, the three phases (6 mini, 18 sanctioned, 24 blind; 1, 1 and 2 lost), 48 episodes, 80 sessions, RORY at 35, eight wanted, one Forge, five systems.
2. **A segment's own XP on the one rule.**
   - `event_xp` pays a clean hit 1, a counter 2, a flash 3 and a drop he takes 10. It pays nothing for a hissatsu, a foul, a hit on your fighter or your own drop.
   - R1 of a delegated dual (the audit's PRIYA against Alfie Crane) paid 81 for events worth 49, +25 won, at ×1.1.
   - The sample's won spar measured 54.
3. **The faucet through the sim's own paths.** Arc 1's first phase, paid with a segment worth 40 won and 10 lost, equals the economy table's arithmetic to the unit in every currency:
   - 6 duals (5 won), 50 scraps (45 won), 15 spars (11 won), 10 Mitts slots at 5 Beans;
   - RORY's XP, each payment at ×1.1.

   All three phases run 48 episodes and 80 sessions, and the level climbs through them.
4. **The sinks priced from the live tables.**
   - The gym: 6,750 HBP and 250 Spirits. A Forge: 40 Spirits and 30 Beans.
   - The audit's PRIYA: Spirits 95 (the ascensions), Beans 100 (her raises), HBP 800 (Chinook Rush and Landslide on the t2 shelf). Budget less what is left would read her Spirits as 85.
   - The squad wants Spirits 1,050 and HBP 13,150. Prestige is wanted nowhere.
5. **The verdict names the currency.** On the sample above the audit is RED and names all five. With every bar opened it says GREEN.

**Gates moved:** none. The segment's XP now flows through `pay_segment` and `event_xp`, and every smoke and determinism line is as it was.

**Determinism and the audit:**
- `-- determinism` adds the audit reduced: the sample's spar and Arc 1's first phase, identical twice to the last number and the spar's hash; a phase one episode longer pays differently.
- **`-- faucets` on this build: RED, all five currencies named.** The playthrough pays Spirits 900, HBP 3,040, Beans 770 and Prestige 1,140 by Arc 3's start; RORY stands at level 21 (22,104 XP).
  - **XP:** RORY reaches level 21, where NP_11 §2's ~35 wants 58,800 XP. The obsolescence audit builds at 35.
  - **Spirits:**
    - 900 of the 1,050 wanted (86 %).
    - 11.2 a session against NP_11 §6's 3–8: `economy.v1.json` pays 3–8 a fight, and a session holds a dual's share and a spar and a half.
  - **HBP:**
    - 3,040 of the 13,150 wanted (23 %).
    - 38 a session against 50–150. NP_02 §4's quests and the story pay nothing on the sim; only wins do.
    - The obsolescence audit's 3,000 a fighter for its three is 9,000.
  - **Beans:** 770 of the 830 wanted (93 %). The wanted raises are the obsolescence audit's jar, a first draft; NP_11 §6 calls Beans a long tail.
  - **Prestige:** 1,140 by Arc 3 into no live sink. NP_11 §6's scouting pulls and open-mat priority are not built.

  The fixes are PAC-098's, the audit their acceptance test. Since C52 all five are made and the verdict is GREEN.

**Result.**
- **Headless gates.** GREEN on all 24 bosses (421/421 each, 10,104 assertions); determinism 51/51; geometry 5/5. Beyond the five new gates every smoke line is C50's, byte for byte. The determinism log is C50's but for the faucet's line and the count; geometry is line for line C50's.
- **The audits.** `-- faucets` RED (exit 1), with the numbers above; `-- audit` GREEN (exit 0), 3 of 3 viable, as in C50.
- **Compiler and tools.** Margins GREEN; the compiler's selftest 10/10, and the 24 sources recompiled byte-identical to the vault's; sim_test 14/14; the hissatsu audit GREEN.
- **Drives.** All 17 green, as in C50.
- **Soaks.** Zero script errors in all of them:
  - `-- demo growth` (29 steps), `-- demo gear boss=1`, `-- demo hybrid`, and `-- demo mirror` ×3.
  - Every mirror run fought one dual and one mirror spar where C47–C49's runs fought two. The scene seeds from the clock, so a fixed-seed run was made on this build and on C49's code (PAC-095's core and tables). The two matched count for count: two duals, two mirrors, 5 counters, 6 segment KOs, the same rewards. Only the frame loop's tick count differed (49,731 against 49,722).

## C52 — What the faucet audit named, fixed: XP paced to the story, the story's HBP, a session's Spirits, the scouting machine, a faucet-backed budget — PROVEN (`-- faucets` GREEN, `-- audit` GREEN)
**Claim.** Each of the five currencies C51 named is fixed on the economy, and the faucet/sink audit says GREEN. Its verdict logic is unchanged. It now counts the canon session's story beat and quest step, and the machine's price, because both are live. The obsolescence audit's budget is re-derived from what the faucet pays, and at that budget it is still GREEN.

**The fixes.**
- **XP: NP_11 s2's curve paced to the story.**
  - The curve is canon (level 50 at 120k; a story-pace player at ~35 by Arc 3), and the amounts per event and per result were first drafts. So one number carries the calibration: `economy.xp.pace` 2.7, applied to every payment at `_pay_xp`, so the amounts keep their proportions.
  - A dual's reserves take half of the dual's total at the pace once (the total is paid at the pace already).
  - A story-pace captain reaches level 35 (59,276 XP) where he stood at 21.
- **HBP: the story and the quests pay (NP_02 s4; NP_11 s6's "quests, wins, story").**
  - `story_mark(flag)` is the overworld's verb when a story beat or quest step lands. It puts the flag on the ledger and pays by kind: a story beat 50, a quest step 30, any other flag nothing. The same flag twice is refused.
  - A seeded flag (`-- flag=`) is the ledger as it stood and pays nothing.
  - The playthrough's session holds NP_02 s5's story beat and its recruitment step (read as a quest step): 118 HBP a session, 72 % of the wanted.
- **Spirits: NP_11 s6's 3–8 read per session.** A spar pays 2 either way, a dual 5 or 8. That is 7.7 a session and 58 % of the wanted, where the per-fight read paid 11.2.
- **Prestige: the scouting machine and the open mat (NP_08 s2.4; NP_11 s6).**
  - A pull costs 10 Prestige and dispenses a lead card (silhouette, House hint, one of NP_02 s1.1's districts), never a person.
  - Every 10th pull is rare-tier. It is a named recruit the club has not met (the recruit table's, never excepted), who turns approachable with the lead. Once they are all met, it is the String Wall's lead the docs give a condition (Vera Iron), turned face up.
  - The other pulls are generics (NP_04 s9's assembler, the tier by the circuit's flags) waiting on the open mat. One signs into the stable free on an open-mat day (every 7th), or any day for the priority (10 Prestige). The stable holds 100 (NP_08 s1).
  - The draws are the machine's own seeded ones, never the fight's RNG. The ledger (`rewards.scouting`) rides the save, and a signed generic rides the save's stable.
  - The wanted is every rare lead the machine holds at the club's start (14) at the pity's pulls: 1,400, of which the faucet affords 81 % (83 % since C53, the story-pace bench's scout counted).
- **Beans, and the obsolescence budget.**
  - The obsolescence audit's jar was a hand-set 100. Its budget is now a fighter's share of what the faucet pays by Arc 3, over the eight dressed, at the 70 % target, rounded: Spirits 110, Beans 140, HBP 1,700 (was 95 / 100 / 3,000).
  - The wanted raises follow the jar: Beans stands at 68 %. The three earliest stay viable at the new budget.

**Mechanism.**
- **The tables** (both copies):
  - `economy.v1.json`: `xp.pace`, the spar's Spirits, `hbp.story` and `hbp.quest`, `prestige.pull` and `prestige.pity`, `scouting` (the districts, the open mat's cadence and priority, the stable's cap, the tier by flags);
  - `audit.v1.json`: the budget and its derivation;
  - `faucets.v1.json`: the session's `story_beats` and `quest_steps`, the wanted Prestige's reading.
- **The sim** (`core.gd`):
  - `_pay_xp`'s pace, `_pay_dual`'s reserve;
  - `story_mark`;
  - `scout_rares`, `scout_why`, `scout_pull`, `_scout_tier`, `open_mat_day`, `open_mat`, `sign_why`, `sign_generic`, `scouting_lines`;
  - the OVER inputs `scout` and `sign`.
- **The audit** (`faucets.gd`): the session's beats and quest steps through `story_mark`; the machine's wanted Prestige priced from `scout_rares` and the table; the Prestige share once it has a sink.
- **The scene** (`main.gd`):

## C53 — The stable roles: a training partner, a scout and a cornerman-in-training worked from the bench, each on the sim's own ledgers — PROVEN
**Claim.** NP_08 §1's rule holds on the sim: "any stablemate can be assigned as a *training partner*, *scout*, or *cornerman-in-training*. Nobody rides the bench doing nothing." A role is NP_11 §9's `stable_role`. It is set between duals on a stable card that is not dressed, rides the reward ledger and the save, and works while the card sits on the bench; dressed, it waits. Each role pays through a path the sim already had: the training slot, the day's turn, and a staffer's use. The faucet/sink audit counts a story-pace bench and stays GREEN.

**The roles.**
- **Training partner** (NP_08 §1: "Beans bonus keyed to their stats"; NP_11 §7: "partner choice adds their stat flavor").
  - A training slot that names no partner takes along the club's training partner with the most to add.
  - He adds a Bean for every 10 points he leads the trainee by at the game's stat, 3 at most, typed as that stat. The game's stat is its bias; for Shadow Rounds' "any" it is the stat he leads by most.
  - A partner no better than the trainee adds nothing. A partner along without the role still gives the quarter's flavour, as before.
- **Scout** (NP_08 §1: "passive Prestige from their home district"; NP_10 §3: "order-confidence % on clubs from that scout's district").
  - When the day turns, each district the bench's scouts cover pays 1 Prestige. A district pays once, however many scouts it has.
  - A scout whose home is one of the circuit's districts reads the order of that district's clubs at +20 on every slot of the projection. The best scout or stand-in counts: Priya's binders stay C27's 30 on Toronto.
- **Cornerman-in-training** (NP_08 §1: "feeds the staff system"; NP_10 §6: "staff level via use").
  - At a dual's end each trainee is one use for the dressed staffer below Lv3 with the fewest uses, else for the corps' next one. His level reads the use as it would a spent charge.
- **Home districts** (NP_11 §9 `Fighter.home_district`).
  - A generic's home is the district the machine drew his lead in (NP_02 §1.1's Harbourline).
  - A House card's home is the House's root (NP_07 §8) on the circuit's districts: YURI the prairies, SAM the north, DEX the maritimes, MARTA Montréal.
  - A houseless card's home is Millbrook Secondary.
- **The aid** (key 7) sets the first bench card without a role: a training partner if the club has none, a scout if nobody covers his district yet, else a cornerman-in-training. Shift+7 sends the last one set back to the ring.

**Mechanism.**
- **The tables** (both copies):
  - `economy.v1.json` gains `roles`: the partner's 10 and 3, the scout's 1 and 20, the trainee's 1 use, and the home districts (the houseless default and twelve Houses' roots). Its notes gain a sentence.
  - `faucets.v1.json` gains `pace.bench`, the story-pace bench. Its pace text and its Prestige system line gain "since" clauses.
- **The sim** (`core.gd`):
  - the roles' block: `stable_role`, `role_working`, `role_holders`, `home_district`, `district_label`, `partner_bonus`, `partner_pick`, `scout_districts`, `_scouts_pay`, `_trainee_mentor`, `_trainees_feed`, `role_why`, `role_pick`, `assign_role`, `role_back`, `role_text`, `roles_lines`;
  - `train` takes the partner along and pays his bonus; `new_day` pays the scouts; `_pay_dual` feeds the trainees;
  - `_staff_credit`'s use is now `_staff_add_use`, shared with the trainees. The charge's own event is unchanged.
  - The projection's scout source reads the bench's scouts.
  - The machine's generic card carries its district.
  - The OVER inputs `role` and `day`; `sign` takes a count.
- **The save** (`save.gd`): each Fighter record carries `stable_role` (the ledger's; it was always "FIGHTER") and `home_district`, and a generic's own `district` rides the card. The roles ride the reward ledger.
- **The audit** (`faucets.gd`):
  - From the circuit, the story-pace bench is three of the machine's generics, one to each role, at the tier of the club phase's pulls.
  - The bench is kept through Arc 3 and paid through the same paths.
  - Each phase reports what the roles paid, and the report gains a bench line.
- **The scene** (`main.gd`):
  - DUAL OVER's ROLES lines; the keys 7 and Shift+7;
  - the ROLE, THE SCOUTS and THE CORNER toasts; the training toast names the partner's bonus;
  - `-- demo roles` (a day turns at each dual's end) and the soak's summary.
- **The drive:** `tools/drive_roles.gd`.

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-099 stage: 7 assertions.
1. **The roles as data.**
   - NP_11 §9's four; the partner's 10 / 3, the scout's 1 / 20, the trainee's 1; twelve Houses.
   - The Millbrook eight at home: four at Millbrook Secondary, SAM the north, DEX the maritimes, YURI the prairies, MARTA Montréal.
   - A machine generic's home is his own district. Every card is a FIGHTER.
2. **The roles set.**
   - Refused: RORY dressed, a role unknown, a card not in the stable, a working bench, the same role twice, mid-fight.
   - The aid's picks down a bench of YURI and three generics: YURI the partner, HILL 1 and RINK 2 scouts, HILL 3 (hill already covered) in the corner.
   - Shift+7's back to the ring.
3. **The training partner against its absence.** JUNE at the heavy bag, 5 Beans at grade 0.5:

`-- determinism` gains one line: the delegated log with the aid pulling, signing, training and turning a day between duals, and with the roles set. It is identical twice, and different from the same log without the roles.

## C54 — The locker room: the stable browsed, a card compared, and a release that frees the place, waits three days and is remembered — PROVEN
**Claim.** NP_08 §4 holds on the sim: "browse by House crest wall, Style, Element, bond web, or slot-role. Comparison card: stats, techniques, trait, dialect, bond lines. Release (rare, reversible for 3 in-game days) frees cap space — released characters return to their district, remain re-recruitable, and remember (a small grudge/warmth flag; people aren't cartridges)." NP_08 §1's cap of 100 now has its valve: the open mat and a recruit are both refused at the cap, and a release frees a place.

**The locker room.**
- **The release.** A bench card is let go between duals.
  - The place is freed at once: the role is cleared, the gear goes back to the club's locker, and the card comes off the Gym Wall.
  - The ledgers stay under the name (XP, growth, techniques, bonds).
  - Refused: the story's four (NP_12A's Millbrook, the captain first; NP_02 §1.1: "captain always slotted"), a dressed card, a card not in the stable, a card with no road back, and mid-fight.
- **Three days.** Before the third day turns, the release can be taken back. The card, the role, the gear and the wall return as they were, and the release leaves no memory. At the third day the release stands:
  - a recruit goes back to the district, approachable again;
  - a generic goes back to the open mat.
- **The flag** is set as the card leaves: warmth with a bond at Lv1 or more to a card who stays, else a grudge. It changes the road back:
  - A warm recruit answers the approach at once. A grudging one wants the condition again and a spar won against the card since the release.
  - A warm generic signs back free any day. A grudging one costs the priority even on an open-mat day.
  - A card that comes back returns as it left (its card dict, `surge_tier` and all), and remembers: the flag rides the Fighter record.
- **The browse** groups the stable by House, Style, Element, the bond web (each card's bonds) or slot-role. **The comparison card** shows a card's House, Style, Element, home and role; the grown stats; the card's own move and kit; the trait; the dialect (the House's, NP_07 §4); and the bonds.

**Mechanism.**
- **The table** (both copies): `economy.v1.json` gains `locker` (the three days, the story's four kept, the flag's Lv1, the five browse orders), and its notes gain a sentence.
- **The sim** (`core.gd`):
  - the locker's block: `_road_back`, `release_flag`, `release_why`, `release_pick`, `release`, `take_back_why`, `take_back`, `_releases_settle`, `home_district_of_card`, `memory_of`, `card_view`, `locker_browse`, `locker_lines`;
  - `new_day` settles the releases three days old;
  - `approach` refuses a card still waiting on a release; `recruit` refuses at the cap, brings a released card back as it left and keeps its memory; `recruit_view` shows the warmth or the grudge's clause;
  - the open mat's price reads a returning card's flag (`_sign_price`), and its line marks the card;
  - the OVER inputs `release` and `take_back`.
- **The save** (`save.gd`): the releases waiting and the memories ride the reward ledger; a Fighter record carries its `memory`.
- **The scene** (`main.gd`):
  - DUAL OVER's LOCKER ROOM lines; 8 (the browse), 9 (the next card), 0 (release the focused card), Shift+0 (take the latest back);
  - the RELEASED, TAKEN BACK and GONE toasts;
  - `-- demo locker` (a day turns on each visit between duals and at each dual's end) and the soak's summary.
- **The drive:** `tools/drive_locker.gd`.

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-100 stage: 6 assertions.
1. **The release against its absence.**
   - HILL 1 is a scout wearing the club's tape wraps, with 500 XP. Released, the stable goes 8 → 7, the wraps are free, the card is off the wall and the role is gone. The 500 XP stays, the flag is a grudge, and the undo runs to day 4.
   - Refused: RORY and JUNE (the story's own), a card not in the stable, SAM dressed on the sanctioned dual, a walk-on with no road back, mid-fight.
2. **Three days to take it back.**
   - Taken back on day 3: the card, the scout's role, the wraps and the wall as they were, no memory, the stable 8.
   - Released again on day 3, it waits through days 3–5 and settles on day 6. Taking it back is then refused, HILL 1 is on the open mat with the grudge, and the memory reads grudge, once.
3. **The road back and the flag.**
   - YURI with a grudge is approachable again, and no rare lead of the machine's. The condition shows BEAT_IN 1/1 and the grudge's spar 0/1, and the recruit is refused. With the spar won YURI joins, `surge_tier` and all, and the record and the comparison card remember the grudge.
   - YURI with a Lv1 bond to RORY answers the approach at once (WARMTH).
   - On the open mat, HILL 3 (bonded) signs back free on day 4. HILL 1 (a grudge) is refused on the open-mat day with no Prestige and signs for the priority's 10.
4. **The cap given its valve.** At 100 the open mat and a recruit are refused. One card released, the open mat signs again.
5. **The browse and the comparison card.**
   - The five browses of the test bench (the mini's four dressed, YURI and three generics).
   - YURI's card: House Kowalchuk, SLUGGER, GRANITE, the prairies, a training partner; PWR 80 …; Grain Elevator; Load-Bearing; dialect kowalchuk; bonds RORY Lv1.
6. **The locker in the save.** A release waiting and a memory ride the save; YURI's record carries the memory. A load takes HILL 1 back and writes back byte-equal.

`-- determinism` gains one line: the delegated log with the aid pulling, signing and turning a day between duals, and releasing the bench's last. It is identical twice, and different from the same log without the releases.

## C55 — District rep: a scrap won in a district raises it, a flee costs it, REP() reads it, and its milestones pay Prestige — PROVEN
**Claim.** NP_02 §2.1 and §4 and NP_08 §2.2 and §2.4 hold on the sim. "Fleeing: allowed pre-engagement freely; mid-scrap flee costs small district rep (−1) — rep gates some recruit conditions." District rep is a gate (NP_02 §4) and, at its milestones, a Prestige source (NP_08 §2.4). The sim now keeps a rep ledger for each district. Carter Okafor's REP(rink_district, 3) and the Whitecloud blessing's REP(the_hill, 3) are met by scraps won there, no longer by a seed alone. The faucet/sink audit counts the story-pace scraps' districts and stays GREEN, its Prestige at the band's edge.

**District rep.**
- **A scrap in a district.** The overworld places a scrap (`start_scrap`'s district; `-- district=` on the scene). A scrap won there raises the district's rep by 1, after the scrap's own pay.
  - A scrap the overworld places nowhere moves no rep: the greybox's older scraps are unplaced.
  - The districts are NP_02 §1.1's, keyed as the machine keys them. The docs' other names (`rink_district`, `the_hill`) are aliases.
- **The flee** (BACKSPACE; the step's `flee` input, delegated or not). A scrap can be walked away from; a spar and a dual are fought out.
  - The scrap ends unpaid, with no PI tick and no BEAT_IN.
  - Before the first exchange it is free. After it, the district loses a point of rep, never going under 0.
- **REP(district, n)** reads the ledger (the recruit grammar's REP is now the sim's). A seeded `rep:` flag still counts. A rep change re-checks the recruits and the houses at once.
- **The milestones** (3, 10 and 25) pay 1, 1 and 2 Prestige the first time the district's rep reaches each. A district that falls and climbs back is not paid twice.
  - The numbers are small on purpose. NP_11 §6's Prestige "5–15 (scraps, rep)" a session is scraps and rep together, and a story-pace session's scraps already pay 13.5 of it.
- **Between duals**, the DISTRICT REP line shows every district's rep and its next milestone.

**Mechanism.**
- **The tables** (both copies):
  - `economy.v1.json` gains `rep`: the scrap's 1, the flee's 1, the milestones, the aliases.
  - `recruits.v1.json`'s grammar makes REP the sim's, with its text and a line in the notes.
  - `faucets.v1.json`'s phases gain `districts` (Ep1's Millbrook Secondary and community centre; the circuit's Rink District and harbour; Arc 2's Hill and downtown strip), and its pace text a "since" clause.
- **The sim** (`core.gd`):
  - the rep's block: `rep_key`, `rep_of`, `_rep_add` (the milestones, a recruit re-check), `flee_why`, `flee`, `rep_lines`;
  - `start_scrap`'s district;
  - `_pay_bout` raises the district's rep after the scrap's toast;
  - `_clause_state`'s REP case;
  - the step's `flee` input, ahead of the delegated profile.
- **The audit** (`faucets.gd`): each phase's scraps are fought in its districts in turn. The rep by Arc 3 and the milestones' Prestige are reported, and the report gains a district rep line.
- **The scene** (`main.gd`): `-- district=`; BACKSPACE; the head's district and rep; the OVER screen's DISTRICT REP line; the REP and FLED toasts; the soak's rep summary.
- **The drive:** `tools/drive_rep.gd`.

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-101 stage: 5 assertions.
1. **District rep, as data and on the ledger.**
   - The table's 1 / 1 / milestones; `the_hill` → hill; REP the sim's.
   - Three tier-1 scraps won against Carter in the Rink District: rep 3 (by alias too) and 7 Prestige, the milestone's 1 among it.
   - Placed nowhere: 6 Prestige and no rep ledger. A scrap lost moves nothing.
   - Fled to 2 and won back to 3: no second milestone.
2. **The flee.**
   - Before the first exchange: free, the scrap over, no XP, no Prestige, no PI, a `fled` verdict.
   - After it, on the step's input: the rink 5 → 4. At 0 it stays 0.
   - Refused: a spar, a dual, a scrap already over.
3. **REP() on the ledger.**
   - Carter approached, three scraps won against him placed nowhere: BEAT_IN 3/3, REP 0/3.
   - Three more in the Rink District: REP 3/3, and he would join (`recruit_ready`).
   - A seeded `rep:rink_district:3` flag still meets it. The Whitecloud blessing's REP(the_hill, 3) turns on the Hill's scraps.
4. **The rep in the save.** The ledger rides the SaveGame; a scrap in flight in the Rink District restores to the same hash with its district. A flee after the load costs the rink its point.
5. **The faucet's districts against their absence.**
   - The rep by Arc 3: school 23, centre 22, rink 68, harbour 67, hill 90, downtown 90. The milestones pay 20 Prestige.
   - Prestige is 1,184 against 1,164 without the districts (85 % against 83 %; 14.8 a session against 14.6). Every other currency is the same.
   - The verdict is GREEN both ways.

`-- determinism` gains one line: SCR-15 on the profile against the live Yuri, in the Rink District with 5 on the ledger. Won, the rink is 6; fled at tick 600, 4; placed nowhere, 5. Each run is identical twice, and the three differ.

**Gates moved — flag for @heat.**
- **C29's recruitment-as-data gate:** the sim now reads seven forms and the overworld four (REP moved).
- **C51's phase arithmetic:** Prestige includes the phase's rep milestones (Arc 1's club phase 135 → 139).
- **Printed only:**
  - C51's verdict report is 18 lines (was 17; the district rep line);
  - C53's bench gate reads 1,184 vs 1,160 (was 1,164 vs 1,140);
  - C51's reduced determinism line reads 139;
  - the content stamp.
- No drive moved.

**Finding — flag for @heat.** Prestige now stands at 85 % of its want (84.6 %, 14.8 a session): the band's edge.
- NP_08 §2.4's third source, circuit placements, cannot be paid at story pace without leaving the band.
- The want would have to grow with the content (NP_08 §9's 60 named at ship against the recruit table's 13), or the scrap's Prestige be read lower. The scrap table's own line is t1 = 1, t2 = 2, t3 = 3, against C22's formula of 2 / 3 / 3, which follows NP_12A's "+2 Prestige".

**Result.**

  - Against C54 the smoke's diff is the same on every boss: the five new gates, the gates moved above and the content stamp.
  - The determinism log is C54's but for C51's reduced line (139), the rep's new line and the count. Geometry is line for line C54's.

  - `-- demo locker`, `-- demo roles`, `-- demo gear boss=1`, `-- demo hybrid`, `-- demo scouting` and `-- demo mirror`.

## C56 — The anti-you: the academy signs the club's beaten rivals when an arc turns, fields them ahead of its prodigies, and gives them back when beaten — PROVEN
**Claim.** NP_12 §2 holds on the sim: "Director Vey, Toronto Ascendant Academy … recruits YOUR beaten rivals between arcs — the anti-you."
- When an arc turns (its story flag landing through `story_mark`), the academy signs up to three rivals the club beat and left waiting: the recruit table's fighters flagged approachable or approached and never signed. A release that has stood (C54) is one of them.
- Signed, a rival is out of the club's reach. Fielded (C46), it fights for the academy ahead of the prodigies with a prodigy's syntheses, Vey in the corner.
- The academy beaten in a dual, every rival it holds comes over, approachable again (NP_12 §6: the academy absorbed into Team Canada).

**The anti-you.**
- **The turns.** `story:nationals` (Arc 1's end, NP_12 §4 beat 7) and `story:hollow_circuit` (Arc 2's), each signing once.
  - A story beat that turns no arc signs nobody. A seeded flag is the ledger as it stood and signs nobody (as a seeded flag pays nothing, C52).
  - The academy signs in the order the club met them, three a turn.
- **Signed.** The rival's recruit record turns "academy", keeping what it was.
  - The approach is refused, naming the academy and the turn. The machine never deals it as a rare lead, and a later win never flags it again.
  - The RECRUITS lines name it, the turn, the day, and a release's grudge or warmth. The String Wall shows it "at the academy".
- **Fielded.** The rivals it holds with a pattern of their own take the squad's first slots on their own patterns, the prodigies after them.
  - Each carries the syntheses of the prodigy whose slot it takes: synthesis without bonds.
  - A signed recruit with no pattern (Dex's card) is held, not fielded, and its line says so.
- **Beaten.** A dual won with the academy fielded brings every rival it holds over: approachable, the turn remembered, a release's memory kept (a grudge still asks its spar, C54). A loss keeps them there.

**Mechanism.**
- **The table** (both copies): `academy.v1.json` gains `recruiting` (the two turns, three a turn), and its notes gain a sentence.
- **The sim** (`core.gd`):
  - the anti-you's block: `academy_taken`, `academy_left_behind`, `_academy_recruit`, `_academy_beaten`, `academy_fielded`, `academy_kit`, `academy_lines`;
  - `story_mark` turns the arc;
  - `_recruit_after_dual` gives the academy's rivals back when it is beaten;
  - the enemy lineup lays the fielded rivals over the prodigies' first slots. With none signed, the academy dresses as before;
  - the segment's setup gives a fielded rival its kit;
  - `approach` refuses a rival at the academy; `recruit_lines` leads with the academy's; the String Wall reads "academy";
  - the OVER inputs `story` and `academy`.
  - The ledger keeps the signing order: a save writes a dictionary's keys sorted.
- **The scene** (`main.gd`): the ANTI-YOU and ACADEMY BEATEN toasts; the RECRUITS lines; `-- demo anti` and the soak's summary.
- **The drive:** `tools/drive_anti.gd`.

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-102 stage: 5 assertions.
1. **The anti-you against its absence.**
   - Yuri's squad beaten flags four, one of them approached. `story:regionals` signs nobody.
   - `story:nationals` signs YURI, JEAN-GUY and SAM in the order met: their states "academy", the fourth still approachable.
   - The turn again is refused. The approach is refused, naming the academy. No rare lead of the machine's.
   - A seeded Nationals flag signs nobody; neither does the table without `recruiting`.
2. **A released rival at the academy.** YURI released from the bench with a grudge stands, then is signed at the turn. The RECRUITS line names the academy, the day and the grudge; the String Wall reads "academy".
3. **The academy's squad with the rivals it signed.**
   - In the mini, the three fill R1–R3. On a sanctioned dual, JEAN-GUY, ADA and CARTER take R1–R3 and ASCENDANT 4 and 5 follow.
   - Vey is in the corner. R1 carries ASCENDANT 1's three syntheses, and R2's kit is ASCENDANT 2's.
4. **The academy beaten.** Lost, it keeps the three. Won, they come over approachable, the turn remembered. YURI's approach still asks the grudge's spar.
5. **The academy in the save.** The signings ride the ledger in their order. A load holds them, refuses the approach and writes back byte-equal.

`-- determinism` gains one line: the delegated log against Moira's squad, the win flagging four. Between duals the aid approaches, the arc turns and the academy is fielded with its signings (MOIRA, DEX, REMY). It is identical twice, and different from the same log without the turn.

**Gates moved — flag for @heat.** None. The content stamp is the only older line that moved (the table changed). Every other smoke, determinism and geometry line is C55's.

  - The determinism log is C55's but for the anti-you's new line and the count. Geometry is line for line C55's.

## C57 — Vendor tiers: the shops' shelves open by the Downtown Strip's rep as well as by the story — PROVEN
**Claim.** NP_02 §4 holds on the sim: district rep gates vendor tiers ("District rep | Gates | Recruit conditions, vendor tiers").
- Both shops stand on the Downtown Strip (NP_02 §1.1: "Shops (HBP spending)"). A t2 or t3 shelf opens by its story flag, or by DOWNTOWN's rep at 10 / 25.
- The shelf reads the rep as it stands: a flee that drops it below the line shuts the shelf again, and what was bought stays bought.
- At story pace the circuit's flags open every shelf first. The rep opens them early only for a player who works the Strip (NP_02 §1.3: grinding is always legal).

**The tiers.**
- **The shelves.** Each shop's table names its district, and a shelf's `rep` is its second key. A shelf with no flag stands open as found; one with no rep opens by its flag only.
  - The rep is the ledger's, or a seeded rep flag's when higher (as REP() counts it, C55).
- **The club's side.** DUAL OVER's SHOPS lines name each shelf, how it stands open and what a shut one wants. A refused purchase names the rep beside the flag. The REP toast names a shelf the rep opened; the FLED toast one it closed.
- **The aid.** The aid's picks (C45's, C48's) read the shelves as they stand, so a shelf the rep opened sells to the aid too.
- **The audit.** `-- faucets` names each shelf's key at story pace and the phase it opened in, the district's rep by phase, and any shelf the rep opened ahead of its beat.

**Mechanism.**
- **The tables** (both copies each): `techniques.v1.json` and `gear.v1.json` gain the shop's `district` and `text` and t2's and t3's `rep`; each notes gains a sentence.
- **The sim** (`core.gd`):
  - the vendor block: `vendor_shop`, `vendor_rep`, `shelf_open_by`, `shelves_open`, `shelf_label`, `shelf_rep_clause`, `vendor_lines`;
  - `shop_open` and `gear_shelf_open` read `shelf_open_by`; their refusals add the rep clause;
  - `_rep_add` names the shelves a move opened or closed on the rep's event and returns them; the flee's event names those it closed;
  - `seed_rep` (`-- rep=`).
- **The audit** (`faucets.gd`): each shelf's first phase open and its key; the report's vendor line.
- **The scene** (`main.gd`): `-- rep=`; the SHOPS lines; the REP and FLED toasts' shelves; `-- demo vendors` and the soak's summary.
- **The drive:** `tools/drive_vendors.gd`.

**Test.** `-- smoke` PAC-103 stage: 5 assertions.
1. **The tiers against their absence.**
   - Both tables read: DOWNTOWN, 10 and 25. At DOWNTOWN 9 with no flag only t1 stands open, and a t2 refusal names the rep.
   - A scrap won downtown makes 10: both t2 shelves open by the rep, named on the rep's event; t3 stays shut.
   - The Rink District's 30 opens nothing; without the tables' rep, DOWNTOWN's 30 opens nothing. A seeded rep flag opens; the Regionals open t2 by the flag at 0. `-- rep=` reads the docs' names by alias.
2. **A shelf opened on the rep and closed below it.**
   - At 10 RORY buys Snowblind off t2 and the club Leather Gloves.
   - A downtown scrap fled after the first exchange: 9, both t2 shelves close, named on the flee's event. Gel Wraps is refused naming the rep; what was bought stays.
   - A scrap won: 10, and both open again.
3. **The shops' lines and the aid.** The SHOPS lines word for word at DOWNTOWN 9 after the Regionals and at 25 with no story. The aid's gear comes off t3 at 25 and off t1 without the rep; the technique shop's pick is t3's (Thunder Bay).
4. **The tiers in the save.** DOWNTOWN 10 rides the ledger; the load reads t2 open by the rep and writes back byte-equal.
5. **The faucet's vendor tiers.**
   - At story pace all four shelves open by their flags in the circuit phase; DOWNTOWN is 0 until Arc 2 (90 by Arc 3).
   - With the club phase's scraps on the Strip, all four open by the rep in the club phase (DOWNTOWN 45). XP, Spirits, HBP and Beans are paid alike.

`-- determinism` gains one line: SCR-15 on the profile with DOWNTOWN at 9 and 600 HBP. Won downtown, the aid's gear between bouts comes off t2 (the Fitted Guard); placed in the rink, off t1. Each run is identical twice, and the two differ.

## Scoring canon (correction)

Greybox v1–v5 ended a dual on any three drops. NP_01 §3 says otherwise: DROP 1, FLASH DROP 2, SEGMENT KO 3 (three knockdowns in one segment, or a failed count), cumulative across the dual, most points wins, EP breaks ties; a KO forfeits the loser's remaining time and the winner shadowboxes it out for +1 Tension per 10 s. v6 implements the canon and the smoke asserts it (DROP +1, FLASH DROP +2, third drop = KO +3 with the dual continuing into the corner). The second knockdown in a segment removes one beat of standing-eight forgiveness (NP_03 §9).

---

## What this pass does NOT claim

- No cross-platform determinism (float math is engine-native).
- No netcode. The architecture admits rollback; it does not ship it.
- No human-validated tuning. C4 is design margin, not playtest data. The random scripted fighter of the determinism log gets dropped roughly every 20 s and drops the boss about as often — a coverage log, not a balance statement.
- The MODEL cells in the C2 table are consequences of the arc geometry, not sourced boxing consensus; playtests may move a window.
- The PPM/Markov predictor for the tracker is not implemented; the n-gram model stands until a predictor beats it on logged human input, which does not exist yet.
- Fouls are the two geometric kinds above; there is no deliberate-foul input and no DQ (NP_01: DQ is an authored story event only).
- The clinch cue's intervals and the 30 % window rule are design numbers, not playtested.
- Boss-side feints exist only as remix operators (C19): no original pattern carries a feint variant, a forced (bait / punish) string never feints, and no pattern binds the player-feint sensors to a hook yet. The pattern data executes in full since C25: the IDLE lines, the last category, run as each boss's neutral game (49 executed, 3 presentational flags). A Scout Book confidence stamp (Tam's lesson) is not built; Cal's corner assist and Vasile's corner intel are read from the boss's META rather than from player-corner data (NP_10). The tell tier step (150 ms), Cal's drift weight (+25), Deshawn's 150 ms recover, Osei's 500 ms stumble, the herding bonus as a fraction of the step and the transfer at exactly half are design values. The five remix records are first drafts from the roster's remix plans; the two new strings (Carter's "Tape Study", Moreau's "Extracted") are placeholders for the content lane. Yuri's `+ tension_bonus` is the standard +5, not an extra. "His special" for SCR-09 / SCR-17 is read as his interruptible cast or a Loud string the block tags; only Baro's S3 is such a string among the 24.
- Scraps and spars end with their verdict and a `bout_end` event; since C22 the same path pays them (XP, Prestige, Spirits, HBP, a stablemate bond, the PI tick named) — the sizes pay their flat amounts (the trickle, the spar's 40) and not the fight's per-event XP, which only a dual segment credits (an editorial reading of NP_02 §2's "XP trickle"). The mirror spar is not in the greybox as NP_02 §2.2's lab flag (the boss agent runs patterns, not profiles); C44's trial mirror is the fighter's own card on strings assembled for it, not a copy of his habits. NPC cards rode a stand-in boss pattern's strings by style until C24; since it a card marked `generic` fights on strings assembled for it, and the `opponent` boss lends only the slot, the club and the index. The "exchange win" is the sim's existing CENTER exchange rule, not the design's last-clean-hit-before-disengage; the 2 s camera-swing budget for the scrap intro is presentation and not built. SCR-24 depends on the opponent having a corner that shouts, so it is offered only where one does. Since C55 a scrap placed in a district moves its rep, and a scrap can be fled (NP_02 §2.1).
- The profile reads Pattern Intel as one added term on its tell-read roll (C18) and nothing more: it still does not know his strings by name or plan around a page. No broadcast camera or seat selection, no authored hands-on-only segments, no mid-segment hand-over (delegation is a corner call; the Exhibition 2v2's pad passing between its two pairs is C42's). The profile's cadences, mixes, read rates and the PI read bonus are first-draft design values; the gates measure behaviours, not those numbers.
- Pattern Intel (C18) stops at the ledger and its three effects: the scout questline route to PI 3 and the tactician's richer pages (NP_10 §7), the Scout Book as a menu is not built (the Film Room's grind rate, shelf and tape are C43's) (the page is an event and a query; decoy seals are C27's, on the formation screen's projection); the reward economy around it is C22's ledger. Two readings are editorial: a scrap counts only when won, a spar and a dual segment count when fought to their end; "beaten once" is a dual segment won against him, not a spar.
- The Exhibition 2v2 (two fighters a side in the ring at once) is C42's, as two engagements under one bell; it is not a rotation format. The R6 pick runs through the normal 45 s corner rather than a shot clock of its own. The 8-dressed squad, its slots and its 3 staff are sealed from a board since C27 (the greybox's formation screen is text on the HUD); story injury states (§6) are not modelled. The recruits' cards (SAM, DEX, YURI, MARTA) are editorial stats and moves from the library pending the content lane; since C29 they are also recruitable, on their recruitment records.
- The rival corner has no staff (bosses have no cutman), no projection of your order (the decoy seal and the projection are C27's, your read of his), and reads none of your corner choices; enemy relay effects stop at the CHARGED surge (bosses cast by composure, not by bar). Coach tells are events, not Scout Book pages. Club membership for the 24 bosses is an editorial mapping in `coaches.v1.json` (the narrative names the clubs, not every boss's club).
